# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal

## B

### 020
Participating local banks (including CDFI financing entities) will have school support/schools targeted for their needs assessed under green loan basis. A partnership demand analysis for the services was conducted and a demand-driven approach was adopted to ensure that the services are tailored to meet the needs of the clients. The services include but not limited to: financial advisory, business plan development, loan application assistance, and capacity building. US banks will provide technical assistance through the Small Business Administration (SBA) which will include mentoring, training, and support programs aimed at enhancing the capacity of small businesses to access financing from local banks. The SBA will also provide technical assistance to local banks to enhance their capacity to deliver green loans.

### 025
Activity 3.2.2: Green Daily Investment Support Services to SMEs. This area is the CDFI capacity building and investment support services for SMEs. The services include but not limited to: financial advisory, business plan development, loan application assistance, and capacity building. US banks will provide technical assistance through the Small Business Administration (SBA) which will include mentoring, training, and support programs aimed at enhancing the capacity of small businesses to access financing from local banks. The SBA will also provide technical assistance to local banks to enhance their capacity to deliver green loans.

### 030
Activity 3.2.3: MSE, household surveys and evaluations. One goal is to assess the needs, challenges, and opportunities for SMEs and households in accessing finance. This activity will be conducted along with the other activities under this component.

## Reporting Entity

### 050
IFAD will be the Applicant Entity (AE) for the Program. In the capacity of IFAD we will be responsible for the implementation, monitoring, and evaluation of the Program. The AE will ensure that the Program is implemented in accordance with the approved Program Document. The AE will also be responsible for reporting to the GCF on the progress made towards achieving the objectives of the Program.

### 055
To fulfill all functions, IFAD will set up a dedicated Programme Task Force (PTF) consisting of IFAD staff.

## Regional Programme Governance

### 060
The Program will be governed by the Program Board. The Program Board will ensure that the Program is implemented in accordance with the approved Program Document and the GCF requirements. The Program Board will also provide strategic direction to the PTF, including a review of the progress made towards achieving the objectives of the Program.

### 065
The Program Board will be composed of representatives from the RPA, including a representative from each of the four countries where the Program is implemented. The Program Board will meet at least once a year or as needed to ensure that the Program is on track and that any issues are addressed in a timely manner.

### 070
The PTF will be responsible for the day-to-day management of the Program, including the implementation of activities, monitoring, and evaluation. The PTF will also provide technical assistance to the RPA and the countries where the Program is implemented.

#### Project A

- **Activities:**

  - Implement water conservation programs.

  - *Water Access:* Improvement in water quality and availability.

  - Quarterly progress reports.

### 2.19
In the case of a national GCF, the Programme will be managed by national Programme Coordination Units (PCUs). The PCU will be responsible for the overall management of the Programme, including the approval of funding proposals, disbursement of funds, and reporting on results. The PCU will also ensure that the Programme is aligned with the country's climate change strategy and implemented in accordance with the GCF's policies and procedures.

---

### 235
Implementation of Component 3 will commence with the identification of the various types of credit in the digital financial ecosystem, under the coordination and supervision of DfID, alongside input from the National Designated Authority (NDA) and the National Bank of Uganda. This will act as a Whitelist Bank for the GCF implementation. The Whitelist Banks will ultimately help manage the application process for accreditation to the GCF. The Whitelist Banks will also be responsible for the identification and approval of digital financial products that are eligible under the programme.

### 236
Five (5) Whitelist Banks provided by the Whitelist Banks, in tandem with the digital borrowers, will be conducted through a competitive bidding process to identify the most suitable banks for an integrated sub-component. Operating with the lending guidelines and other conditions set out by the GCF, the Whitelist Banks will be responsible for the identification of eligible borrowers, the approval of credit applications, and the disbursement of funds.

### 237
The technical support activities of Sub-Component 3.2 will both the participating banks and the digital financial institutions. This will include the development of a comprehensive training programme by DfID, in collaboration with the National Designated Authority (NDA) and the Whitelist Banks. The training programme will be designed to identify and address any gaps or challenges that may arise during the implementation process.

## Partnerships

### 020
During implementation the Program will foster relevant and specialized government agencies, academic institutions, non-government organizations (NGOs), international bodies, foundations and private sector entities to ensure effective implementation of the Program. The Program will also promote public-private partnerships (PPP) in order to enhance the sustainability of the Program. The Program will be implemented through a participatory approach that involves all stakeholders including local communities, NGOs, government agencies, and other relevant institutions.

### 021
In Kenya, implementation will be supported by KALRO, the Kenya Agricultural and Livestock Research Organization (KALRO), the Ministry of Environment and Forestry, and the National Government Affirmative Action Fund (NGAAF).

### 022
In Uganda, implementation will be supported by GSA, NAGRC&DB, NAARI, the Ministry of Local Government, and the Ministry of Water and Environment.

### 023
In Rwanda, implementation will be supported by RAB, RNSP, REMA, RCGL, the Ministry of Environment, the University of Rwanda, Research Funds, Faculty of Sciences and Technology, and Kigali Institute of Science and Technology (KIST).

### 024
In Tanzania, implementation will be supported by SUA, NARI, NAFA, the Ministry of Agriculture, the Ministry of Livestock, the Ministry of Natural Resources and Tourism, and the Vice President's Office - Union.

## Program Co-Financing

### 030
The Program will also seek co-financing from development partners to support its activities. The amount of co-financing required for each activity will be determined by the specific needs of the activity and the availability of funds. The Program will also explore opportunities for co-financing with other programs and projects funded by the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and other multilateral funding agencies.

## R.R. Justification for GCF funding request

The Programme will be implemented in five countries in East Africa, three of which are classified Least Developed Countries (LDCs). The programme is designed to support the implementation of National Adaptation Plans (NAPs) and other climate change adaptation initiatives at national level. These NDCs are the main subordinates to the common effects of climate change in their respective countries. The Programme will be implemented through a series of activities that include training, capacity building, policy development, and awareness raising on climate change issues. The programme will also support the implementation of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) indicators related to climate change adaptation.

The Programme is expected to cost US$ 1.2 million for a period of three years from 2018 to 2020. This amount includes all costs associated with the implementation of the programme, including personnel costs, travel expenses, and other operational costs.

# EXPECTED PERFORMANCE AGAINST INVESTMENT CRITERIA

## 201. Investment Criteria

The expected outcome is to demonstrate that the proposed project will result in a significant reduction of greenhouse gas emissions and contribute to climate change mitigation by reducing emissions from deforestation and forest degradation, enhancing carbon stocks, and improving livelihoods. The project will also provide additional co-benefits such as biodiversity conservation, improved water quality, and enhanced ecosystem services.

## 208. To achieve emission reductions

The Programme will provide technical assistance for the establishment and implementation of a robust monitoring, reporting, and verification (MRV) system, as well as the provision of adequate incentives to local communities, including landowners, to ensure compliance with the programme's requirements. This will include the development of a robust MRV system that is transparent, reliable, and cost-effective.

## 205. The Programme adopts a participatory approach

The Programme adopts a participatory approach to avoid conflicts between different stakeholders and to enhance the sustainability of the project. The Programme will engage with local communities, including landowners, to ensure their participation in the design and implementation of the programme. This will include the development of a robust MRV system that is transparent, reliable, and cost-effective.

## D.2. Paradigm shift potential

### 255.
The Programme's comprehensive approach to the paradigm shift in the energy sector will reduce GHG emissions by 30% by 2030 and achieve carbon neutrality by 2050. This is achieved through various interventions, including capacity building for climate resilience, promoting renewable energy, and enhancing energy efficiency. The programme also supports the development of sustainable transport systems and promotes the use of clean technologies to reduce emissions.

### 256.
Through its inclusion of key stakeholders in its programmes, the Programme has strong potential for sustainability. It involves local communities and their governments in planning and implementing activities, ensuring that they are owned by those who will benefit from them. This approach also enables the programme to respond effectively to changing circumstances and needs.

### 257.
The local level components of the Programme are expected to contribute significantly to the creation of green jobs and sustainable livelihoods. By providing training and capacity building in renewable energy technologies, the Programme can help to create new employment opportunities for local communities. This will also support the development of a skilled workforce that is equipped to meet the challenges of a changing climate.

### 258.
In addition, the Programme will advance regional, national, and local legislative and regulatory frameworks. A robust legal framework is necessary to support the implementation of the programme's activities and to ensure their sustainability. The programme will also promote the development of policies that support the transition to a low-carbon economy and encourage investment in renewable energy.

### 259.
The Programme contributes significantly to social and health benefits in the targeted regions. It encompasses interventions aimed at improving access to clean water, sanitation, and healthcare services. These interventions are designed to address the needs of vulnerable populations, with a focus on the amelioration of safe and high-quality drinking water, thereby reducing waterborne diseases.

### Sample text highlights based on features of expertise to manage and reduce risk are identified here.

#### 238. The Programmes will be implemented through Technical Assistance, Monitoring, and Payment have the highest number of people affected by flooding with 10,000 to 50,000 people affected each year. Exposure to drought is also high with 70% of the population affected annually.

Certain areas are vulnerable from a geomorphological standpoint such as river flood plains, coastal zones, and low-lying areas. In addition, the impact of climate change on water resources is expected to increase the frequency and intensity of floods. This will lead to an increase in the number of people affected by droughts and floods.

The risk of flooding for cities along major rivers and coastal areas is increasing due to urbanization and land-use changes. This is a critical factor affecting the vulnerability of these areas, particularly in low-lying regions where sea-level rise is expected to exacerbate the problem. In addition, climate change is expected to increase the frequency and intensity of extreme weather events such as hurricanes, typhoons, and cyclones.

#### 271. In addition, poor construction techniques, climate vulnerability, supply, and agriculture extension services are critical factors that need to be addressed in order to reduce the risk of flooding.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V3.0: PAGE 74

## Feasibility

### Low Carbon Transport Project in the Bay Area

The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by promoting low-carbon transport options and improving traffic management practices. Key activities include:

- Implementing electric vehicle charging infrastructure.
- Promoting public transportation use.
- Encouraging cycling and walking.

Daily commuters and professionals will benefit from reduced travel times, lower fuel costs, and improved air quality. The project also aims to create jobs in the green transport sector and stimulate local economic growth.

This in-region project is meeting national climate targets and international commitments. It aligns with the government's sustainable development goals and contributes to regional economic development by creating new job opportunities and stimulating local businesses.

### Replication

The government of the region has committed to replicating this model in other regions, focusing on areas with high traffic congestion and air pollution. The project will serve as a blueprint for similar initiatives across the country.

## Implementation

### Medium

Appropriately recycling waste with a well-designed system is essential for environmental sustainability. The project aims to:

- Reduce waste sent to landfills.
- Create jobs in the recycling industry.
- Promote public awareness of recycling practices.

The program will provide training and resources to local communities, schools, and businesses to implement effective recycling programs. It will also establish partnerships with private sector companies to enhance recycling infrastructure and ensure proper disposal of hazardous materials.

### High

Passing this law would lead to substantial government investment in waste management infrastructure and create new jobs. The project aims to:

- Improve public health by reducing exposure to harmful pollutants.
- Promote sustainable practices among businesses and individuals.
- Enhance the region's reputation as an environmentally responsible jurisdiction.

The program will also provide financial incentives for companies that adopt green technologies, such as solar panels and wind turbines. It will create a comprehensive recycling program that includes education, infrastructure development, and enforcement mechanisms to ensure compliance with regulations.

### Medium

The government will allocate funds to support the implementation of this project. The program will be carried out in phases over five years, with an initial focus on pilot projects in selected regions. This approach allows for monitoring and evaluation of the effectiveness of the program before full-scale implementation.

### High

| Core Indicator | Baseline Context | Rating for Current State | Target Outcome Level | How the Programme will contribute |
|----------------|------------------|-------------------------|----------------------|-----------------------------------|
| **Greenhouse Gas Emissions** | Measured by: GHG emissions per unit of GDP, and GHG emissions from land use, land-use change and forestry (LULUCF). | Not applicable | A 20% reduction in GHG emissions by 2030 compared to the baseline. | Mitigating climate change through improved energy efficiency, renewable energy adoption, and sustainable practices. |
| **Climate Resilience** | Measured by: Number of people affected by disasters per year, and number of deaths due to disasters per year. | Not applicable | A 50% reduction in the number of people affected by disasters by 2030 compared to the baseline. | Enhancing climate resilience through improved infrastructure, early warning systems, and disaster risk management. |
| **Ecosystem Services** | Measured by: Biodiversity indicators (e.g., species richness), water quality indicators (e.g., nutrient levels), and soil health indicators (e.g., organic matter content). | Not applicable | A 30% improvement in ecosystem services by 2030 compared to the baseline. | Conserving biodiversity, improving water quality, and enhancing soil health through sustainable land management practices. |
| **Social Equity** | Measured by: Poverty incidence, access to clean energy, and access to climate information and early warning systems. | Not applicable | A 40% reduction in poverty incidence by 2030 compared to the baseline. | Promoting social equity through improved access to clean energy, climate information, and early warning systems for vulnerable populations. |

### Case Study: E. Degrees

#### Objective:

- Assess the feasibility of a
- wide range of climate change mitigation
- opportunities and technologies.

#### Mitigation:

- Wide range of opportunities to improve
- energy efficiency, renewable energy,
- carbon capture and storage (CCS),
- and other low-carbon technologies.
- Focus on opportunities that are
- cost-effective, scalable, and
- have a significant impact on reducing greenhouse gas emissions.

### Multi-country

Daily fermes as integrated part of the
climate change mitigation strategy. The
strategy includes the development and
implementation of climate change mitigation
and adaptation measures in the region.
Particular focus is given to the development
of renewable energy sources, energy efficiency,
and low-carbon technologies that reduce
greenhouse gas emissions. Special attention
is paid to the integration of climate change
mitigation and adaptation measures into
national policies and strategies.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V3.0: PAGE 84

## E

### 1.1.2. Strengthen local government capacity to implement climate change adaptation and mitigation actions

- **Objective:** To strengthen local government capacity to implement climate change adaptation and mitigation actions.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.1.2.1:** Improve local government technical capacities for climate change planning, implementation, and monitoring.
    - **Action 1.1.2.1.a:** Develop and implement climate change action plans at the local level.
    - **Action 1.1.2.1.b:** Establish early warning systems for climate-related hazards.
    - **Action 1.1.2.1.c:** Enhance data collection, analysis, and dissemination of climate information.
  - **Activity 1.1.2.2:** Strengthen local government financial capacities to support climate change actions.
    - **Action 1.1.2.2.a:** Develop and implement local financing mechanisms for climate change actions.
    - **Action 1.1.2.2.b:** Enhance access to climate finance at the local level.

### 1.3.3. Improve sustainable land management practices

- **Objective:** To improve sustainable land management practices that enhance ecosystem resilience and carbon sequestration.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.3.3.1:** Promote integrated landscape approaches to sustainable land management.
    - **Action 1.3.3.1.a:** Develop and implement integrated landscape management plans.
    - **Action 1.3.3.1.b:** Enhance collaboration among stakeholders in landscape management.
  - **Activity 1.3.3.2:** Support the adoption of sustainable land management practices by smallholders and local communities.
    - **Action 1.3.3.2.a:** Develop and disseminate guidelines for sustainable land management practices.
    - **Action 1.3.3.2.b:** Provide training and technical assistance to smallholders and local communities.

### 1.4.4. Enhance the capacity of national institutions to support climate change actions

- **Objective:** To enhance the capacity of national institutions to support climate change actions.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.4.4.1:** Strengthen the institutional framework for climate change governance.
    - **Action 1.4.4.1.a:** Develop and implement a national climate change policy.
    - **Action 1.4.4.1.b:** Establish a national climate change secretariat.
  - **Activity 1.4.4.2:** Enhance the capacity of national institutions to provide technical assistance and support to subnational governments and communities.
    - **Action 1.4.4.2.a:** Develop and implement training programs for national institution staff.
    - **Action 1.4.4.2.b:** Establish partnerships with international organizations to enhance capacity building.

### 1.5.5. Promote public awareness and participation in climate change actions

- **Objective:** To promote public awareness and participation in climate change actions.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.5.5.1:** Develop and implement public awareness campaigns on climate change.
    - **Action 1.5.5.1.a:** Create educational materials on climate change for the public.
    - **Action 1.5.5.1.b:** Organize public events to raise awareness about climate change.
  - **Activity 1.5.5.2:** Facilitate public participation in climate change decision-making processes.
    - **Action 1.5.5.2.a:** Establish mechanisms for public input in climate change planning and implementation.
    - **Action 1.5.5.2.b:** Provide platforms for public dialogue on climate change actions.

### 1.6.6. Enhance the capacity of national financial institutions to support climate change financing

- **Objective:** To enhance the capacity of national financial institutions to support climate change financing.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.6.6.1:** Develop and implement green finance products and services.
    - **Action 1.6.6.1.a:** Design and launch green bonds and other green finance instruments.
    - **Action 1.6.6.1.b:** Provide technical assistance to financial institutions for green finance development.
  - **Activity 1.6.6.2:** Enhance the capacity of national financial institutions to assess and manage climate risks.
    - **Action 1.6.6.2.a:** Develop and implement climate risk management frameworks.
    - **Action 1.6.6.2.b:** Provide training on climate risk assessment for financial institution staff.

### 1.7.7. Support the development of a green economy

- **Objective:** To support the development of a green economy that promotes sustainable growth and poverty reduction.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.7.7.1:** Promote green technologies and practices in key sectors.
    - **Action 1.7.7.1.a:** Develop and implement policies to promote green technologies.
    - **Action 1.7.7.1.b:** Provide incentives for the adoption of green technologies by businesses.
  - **Activity 1.7.7.2:** Support the development of green industries and job creation.
    - **Action 1.7.7.2.a:** Develop and implement programs to support green entrepreneurship.
    - **Action 1.7.7.2.b:** Provide training and technical assistance to green businesses.

### 1.8.8. Enhance the capacity of national research institutions to support climate change actions

- **Objective:** To enhance the capacity of national research institutions to support climate change actions.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.8.8.1:** Strengthen the research infrastructure for climate change studies.
    - **Action 1.8.8.1.a:** Develop and implement research programs on climate change.
    - **Action 1.8.8.1.b:** Establish partnerships with international research institutions.
  - **Activity 1.8.8.2:** Enhance the capacity of national researchers to provide technical assistance and support to subnational governments and communities.
    - **Action 1.8.8.2.a:** Develop and implement training programs for national researchers.
    - **Action 1.8.8.2.b:** Establish mechanisms for knowledge sharing among researchers, policymakers, and practitioners.

### 1.9.9. Promote regional cooperation on climate change actions

- **Objective:** To promote regional cooperation on climate change actions.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.9.9.1:** Develop and implement regional climate change programs.
    - **Action 1.9.9.1.a:** Establish regional climate change committees.
    - **Action 1.9.9.1.b:** Coordinate regional climate change actions among countries.
  - **Activity 1.9.9.2:** Facilitate the exchange of best practices and lessons learned on climate change actions among regions.
    - **Action 1.9.9.2.a:** Organize regional workshops and conferences on climate change.
    - **Action 1.9.9.2.b:** Develop and disseminate regional climate change case studies.

### 1.10.10. Enhance the capacity of national educational institutions to support climate change education

- **Objective:** To enhance the capacity of national educational institutions to support climate change education.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.10.10.1:** Develop and implement climate change curricula in educational institutions.
    - **Action 1.10.10.1.a:** Integrate climate change topics into existing curricula.
    - **Action 1.10.10.1.b:** Develop new courses on climate change for educational institutions.
  - **Activity 1.10.10.2:** Provide training and technical assistance to educators on climate change education.
    - **Action 1.10.10.2.a:** Develop and implement training programs for teachers on climate change education.
    - **Action 1.10.10.2.b:** Establish partnerships with international educational institutions to enhance capacity building.

### 1.11.11. Support the development of a green transport system

- **Objective:** To support the development of a green transport system that reduces greenhouse gas emissions.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.11.11.1:** Promote the use of public transportation and non-motorized transport.
    - **Action 1.11.11.1.a:** Develop and implement policies to promote public transportation.
    - **Action 1.11.11.1.b:** Provide incentives for the use of non-motorized transport.
  - **Activity 1.11.11.2:** Support the development of electric vehicles and charging infrastructure.
    - **Action 1.11.11.2.a:** Develop and implement policies to promote electric vehicles.
    - **Action 1.11.11.2.b:** Provide incentives for the adoption of electric vehicles by individuals and businesses.

### 1.12.12. Enhance the capacity of national health institutions to support climate change actions

- **Objective:** To enhance the capacity of national health institutions to support climate change actions.
- **Activities:**
  - **Activity 1.12.12.1

## Activity 1.4.2 Establish regional climate change adaptation and mitigation centers

- The proposed center will provide technical assistance to countries in the region for mainstreaming climate change adaptation and mitigation into national development plans.
- The center will also serve as a hub for knowledge sharing, capacity building, and policy dialogue on climate change issues.

## Activity 1.4.3 Establish regional gender equality centers

- The proposed center will provide technical assistance to countries in the region for mainstreaming gender equality into national development plans.
- The center will also serve as a hub for knowledge sharing, capacity building, and policy dialogue on gender equality issues.

## Activity 2.1.1 Strengthen key environmental management and governance institutions

- This activity supports the establishment of regional centers to enhance the capacity of key environmental management and governance institutions in the region.
- The center will provide technical assistance, training, and policy advice to strengthen these institutions.

## Activity 2.1.2 Build and enhance regional cooperation mechanisms

- This activity supports the establishment of regional cooperation mechanisms to enhance regional cooperation on climate change adaptation and mitigation.
- The center will provide technical assistance, training, and policy advice to strengthen these mechanisms.

## Activity 3.1.2 Facilitate sustainable energy management strategies

- This activity supports the development of sustainable energy management strategies in the region.
- The center will provide technical assistance, training, and policy advice to support the implementation of these strategies.

### Section 4: Capacity Building and Training

#### 4.1.1 General capacity building

- A regional program on climate change, gender equality, and environmental governance is proposed.
- This program will include training, workshops, and other capacity-building activities.

#### 4.1.2 Women's empowerment and gender mainstreaming

- A specific program for women's empowerment and gender mainstreaming is proposed.
- This program will include training, workshops, and other capacity-building activities.

#### 4.1.3 Youth engagement and leadership development

- A specific program for youth engagement and leadership development is proposed.
- This program will include training, workshops, and other capacity-building activities.

### Section 5: Monitoring and Evaluation

#### 5.1.1 Monitoring the progress and impact of activities

- Regular monitoring and evaluation of the activities will be conducted to ensure they are achieving their objectives.
- This will include both quantitative and qualitative indicators.

#### 5.1.2 Reporting on results and impacts

- Regular reports on the results and impacts of the activities will be prepared and disseminated.
- These reports will be used to inform decision-making and improve the effectiveness of the activities.

### Section 6: Financial Management

#### 6.1.1 Budgeting and financial planning

- A detailed budget for the program is proposed, including all expected costs.
- This budget will be regularly reviewed and updated as needed.

#### 6.1.2 Financial management and accountability

- The financial management of the program will be conducted in accordance with relevant regulations and standards.
- Regular audits will be conducted to ensure accountability and transparency.

### Section 7: Communication and Outreach

#### 7.1.1 Public awareness campaigns

- A public awareness campaign on climate change, gender equality, and environmental governance is proposed.
- This campaign will include media outreach, social media, and other communication activities.

#### 7.1.2 Stakeholder engagement

- Regular engagement with stakeholders, including governments, civil society organizations, and the private sector, is proposed.
- This engagement will be conducted through consultations, workshops, and other activities.

### Section 8: Risk Management

#### 8.1.1 Identifying and managing risks

- A risk management plan is proposed to identify and manage potential risks to the program.
- This plan will include regular risk assessments and mitigation strategies.

#### 8.1.2 Contingency planning

- A contingency plan is proposed to ensure the continuity of the program in case of unexpected events or disruptions.
- This plan will be regularly reviewed and updated as needed.

### Section 9: Legal and Regulatory Framework

#### 9.1.1 Compliance with relevant laws and regulations

- The program will comply with all relevant laws and regulations, including those related to climate change, gender equality, and environmental governance.
- Regular legal reviews will be conducted to ensure compliance.

#### 9.1.2 Policy dialogue and advocacy

- The program will engage in policy dialogue and advocacy to promote the integration of climate change, gender equality, and environmental governance into national policies and strategies.

## Activity 2.2.1: Promote the adoption of climate-smart agriculture (CSA) practices and technologies to enhance resilience and food security through integrated approaches and cross-sectoral linkages in landscapes.

- Breeding and genetic improvement are an essential part of climate-smart agriculture.
- The use of improved varieties can increase yields by 30% or more under good management.
- Promote the adoption of CSA practices and technologies that enhance resilience to climate change.

## Activity 2.2.2: Provide support for sustainable intensification of agricultural production systems, including integrated nutrient management, agroforestry, and other ecosystem-based approaches.

- Promotion through valued performance-based incentives, not just aid.
- Support smallholder farmers with improved seeds, fertilizers, and storage solutions.
- Encourage the adoption of CSA practices that enhance resilience to climate change.

## Activity 2.2.3: Promote climate-smart water management in agriculture.

- The adoption of climate-smart water management practices is essential for sustainable agriculture.
- Support smallholder farmers with improved irrigation systems and water management techniques.
- Promote integrated water resource management (IWRM) to enhance resilience to climate change.

## Activity 2.2.4: Improve access to finance for climate-smart agriculture.

- Approaches that are successful in providing affordable credit to smallholder farmers include:
    - Group lending schemes
    - Input and output markets
    - Value chain financing

## E.7. Monitoring, reporting and evaluation arrangements

### 300
Each GCF RMO will implement and coordinate the relevant MAE system building on the existing M&E systems if the relevant IPU knowledge is available. The MAE system will be designed to capture data from the relevant IPU and other sources as appropriate. Data captured by the MAE system will include the following:

- **Project-level data:** Project title, project ID, funding window, funding source, etc.
- **Financial data:** Amounts disbursed, committed, and spent; types of financing (e.g., grants, loans).
- **Environmental data:** Greenhouse gas emissions reductions achieved, co-benefits realized.

### 301
The relevant MAE system for the GCF will be aligned with Tac and Logframe. The system will be constructed by full through ongoing management to ensure that it is consistent with the overall M&E framework of the GCF. The GCF will work closely with the IPU, FAD, and the relevant RMO to ensure that the MAE system is aligned with the overall M&E framework.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.0 PAGE 91

## First Commitment: The developed countries will assist the new and existing climate and environment protection projects with investment finance and technical assistance to implement measures in their own countries.

The developed countries will also provide financial resources and technical support to developing countries for the implementation of adaptation measures, including:

- Climate change mitigation
- Adaptation measures
- Technology transfer
- Capacity building

The target countries will be priority and address emerging issues such as climate change, biodiversity loss, desertification, and land degradation. The target countries will also include a section of PRA, at strengthening governance management with the greatest impact.

### Baseline Surveys

The Programme will undertake baseline surveys at the beginning of implementation in all targeted areas. The purpose is to identify the current status of the environment and climate change issues in the area. The baseline survey will be conducted by the local government and community-based organizations (CBOs) with technical support from the implementing agencies.

### Medium-Term Review and Final Evaluation

The Programme will undertake a medium-term review and final evaluation, with the approval and consent of PAC, according to the requirements of the GCF evaluation criteria. The evaluation will be conducted by an independent consultant or organization selected through a competitive process. The evaluation will assess the effectiveness, efficiency, sustainability, and impact of the Programme's contributions to the objectives.

### Knowledge Management, Communication, and Learning

The Programme will establish a M&E and Communication Unit that will oversee Programme implementation with the necessary support to ensure the following knowledge management activities:

- Documentation and dissemination of lessons learned
- Development of best practices
- Sharing of experiences among stakeholders
- Capacity building for local governments and communities
- Regular updates on progress and achievements

The Programme will also include a section on climate change in the annual report. The Programme will be implemented through a decentralized approach, with technical support from the implementing agencies.

# RISK ASSESSMENT AND MANAGEMENT

## F. Risk factors and mitigation measures (page 3 topics)

### Selected Risk Factor 1: Institutional Capacity for Implementation and Sustainability

| Category | Probability | Impact | Mitigation Measures |
|----------|-------------|--------|---------------------|
| Technical and institutional | High | Medium |

208. Under staff exists in the daily visits done in the four countries particularly in areas of food production are limited. The staffs are not well trained on the use of modern technology for food production. The lack of technical support is also a problem.

**Mitigation Measures:**
- 231. In each of the four countries, a Programme management unit will be established by the FAO project that will be responsible for the implementation and supervision of the project activities. This will include providing training on modern technology for food production.
- Continuous capacity building in collaboration with government and FAO staff through exchange visits and joint exercises.

### Selected Risk Factor 2: Financial Management and Financial Risks

| Category | Probability | Impact | Mitigation Measures |
|----------|-------------|--------|---------------------|
| Governance | High | Medium |

209. The Programme activities multiple monitoring and the Project Management Units (PMUs), facilities, survey equipment, and other materials are required to be procured. This requires a lot of funds.

**Mitigation Measures:**
- 308. The project will be implemented in phases. These funds will cover the activities for each phase. The funds will be released on an annual basis.
- The project will be implemented through the Ministry of Finance, and the PMUs will be responsible for the procurement of materials and equipment required by the project.
- Continuous monitoring and evaluation to ensure that the funds are used effectively.

## F

### Section Risk Factor 2: Senior Struggles and Policies

| Category | Probability | Impact |
|----------|-------------|--------|
| **Description** | **Risk** | **Mitigation Measures** |
| **005** - Lack of local expertise and capacity to manage climate change projects. | Low | - Develop local expertise through training and mentoring programs.<br>- Encourage partnerships with international organizations that have experience in climate change management.<br>- Provide financial support for local institutions to build capacity. |

### Section Risk Factor 3: Project Scope and Approach

| Category | Probability | Impact |
|----------|-------------|--------|
| **Description** | **Risk** | **Mitigation Measures** |
| **010** - The project scope is too narrow, failing to address the root causes of climate change. | Medium | - Expand the project scope to include broader community engagement.<br>- Integrate climate change adaptation and mitigation strategies into the project design.<br>- Encourage multi-stakeholder participation in project planning and implementation. |

### Section Risk Factor 4: Technological Constraints

| Category | Probability | Impact |
|----------|-------------|--------|
| **Description** | **Risk** | **Mitigation Measures** |
| **015** - The technology used is not suitable for the local context or is too expensive. | High | - Conduct a thorough assessment of locally available technologies.<br>- Promote the use of low-cost, appropriate technologies that are well-suited to the local environment.<br>- Provide training and support to help communities adopt and maintain new technologies. |

### Section Risk Factor 5: Value Chain Issues

| Category | Probability | Impact |
|----------|-------------|--------|
| **Description** | **Risk** | **Mitigation Measures** |
| **020** - In case the value chain issues impede long-term participation, some additional measures may be needed to ensure sustainability. | Low | - Develop a comprehensive plan for addressing value chain issues.<br>- Engage with local stakeholders to identify and address any barriers to participation.<br>- Provide ongoing support to help communities overcome challenges and build resilience over time. |

### 303
Small barnyard farmers are excluded in important markets because they do not have sufficient access to credit to meet the economic return they need.

#### Mitigation Mechanisms:
- Microfinance

### 304
The Programme has a strong focus on delivering outcomes to benefit for small holders, under Output 2.1, particularly through the provision of credit and inputs (seeds, fertilizers, etc.) and also both participatory approaches that capture the needs of smallholders and community driven development initiatives. The programme will support the establishment of smallholder farmer associations and cooperatives to provide access to markets for their produce.

#### Sector Risk Factor 8: Risk of lack of demand for GCF funds
- Medium

### 305
Establishing fair and equitable small-scale credit lines in a region subject to very severe climate change impacts can be challenging. The Programme will work with the local financial institutions to develop innovative financing mechanisms that are responsive to the needs of smallholders, particularly women and youth.

#### Mitigation Mechanisms:
- Microfinance

The Programme will address issues such as GCF policies, safeguards and instruments. Safeguard environmental and social risk management systems will be developed and implemented in line with the requirements of the GCF and other relevant international standards.

## GCF POLICIES AND STANDARDS

### G.1: Environmental and Social Risk Assessment

#### 307
The Programme will leverage a new tool (IFAD’s Locally Led Environmental and Social Safeguards) to ensure that environmental and social risk assessments are conducted in line with the requirements of the GCF.

Recognizing that nationally appropriate mechanisms may improve the environment and human health during various stages of project implementation, the Programme will work with local communities to develop and implement these mechanisms. The Programme will also work with local communities to ensure that environmental and social risk assessments are conducted in line with the requirements of the GCF.

### G.2: Gender Equality

#### 309
The Programme will promote gender equality by ensuring that women have equal access to resources, services and opportunities. The Programme will also work with local communities to develop and implement mechanisms to ensure that women have equal access to resources, services and opportunities.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.3 | PAGE 8

## 20.1 Community Development Plan

The community development plan constitutes of several activities in different areas and is a key component of the overall project. The main objective of this component is to ensure that the beneficiaries are empowered and able to participate fully in the implementation of the GCF project. The following objectives will be achieved through the implementation of this component:

- To build capacity for community participation.
- To address gender issues and other social concerns.
- To promote environmental conservation.

The CDM addresses gender, and other social concerns, and offers an enabling environment for women to participate fully in the project activities. It also ensures that the needs of vulnerable groups are addressed through the implementation of this component.

## 20.2 Gender Assessment and Action Plan

### 20.2.1 Gender Assessment

The gender assessment will be conducted 2 days (Capaciton #2) prior to the GCF project meeting, which is after it will be posted on the IPAD website for the GCF website. The gender assessment will be conducted by a team of experts in gender issues and will include the following activities:

- To assess the current status of women's participation in the project.
- To identify the barriers to women's participation in the project.
- To develop strategies to address these barriers.

### 20.2.2 Gender Action Plan

The gender action plan will be developed based on the findings of the gender assessment and will include the following activities:

- To increase women's participation in the project.
- To provide training and capacity building for women.
- To promote women's leadership in the project.
- To develop strategies to address the barriers identified in the gender assessment.

## 20.3 The Monitoring Plan

The monitoring plan will be developed based on the objectives of the project and will include the following activities:

- To monitor the progress of the project towards achieving its objectives.
- To collect data on the impact of the project on the community.
- To ensure that the project is implemented in accordance with the approved work plan.

## 20.4 Addressing Social Issues

The Programme will address social issues, the Programme will:

- Increase women economic empowerment through Gary and other activities.
- Promote gender equality and equity in all activities.
- Ensure that all activities are inclusive of all members of the community.
- Promote the rights of vulnerable groups, such as children, people with disabilities, and the elderly.

## 20.5 Financial Management Plan

The financial management plan will be developed based on the approved budget and will include the following activities:

- To ensure that the project is implemented in accordance with the approved work plan.
- To monitor the use of funds and ensure that they are used for their intended purpose.
- To ensure that all procurement activities are conducted in accordance with the approved procurement plan.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.1.0 - Page 8

## GCF funds from IFAD: Solicitation of interest private accounts in local currency will be applied by IFAD. Interest (if any) earned on these accounts will be remitted to the GCF. The accounts will be managed by IFAD and used to implement GCF funded and related loans for their beneficiaries. The accounts will be operated under the supervision of the GCF Secretariat, in accordance with the Board and RMC decisions; all withdrawal applications and PRIs need to be approved by the RMC.

### 315. Disbursement to host a sub-window of private sector investment funds (PRIs) within 36 months after approval of the funding proposal.
- The GCF will provide FAO with a cash pool and add a cash buffer for the next ten years. Any GCF funds that have not been disbursed by the end of each year will be returned to the GCF.

### 316. Accounting and financial reporting requirements (IFAD) - Field Level Monitoring (FLM).
- The FLM system will be implemented in accordance with the IFAD accounting and reporting guidelines. The system will be used to monitor the use of funds by the implementing entities, and to ensure that the funds are spent according to the approved project budgets.

### 317. Disbursement from the accounts to the sub-window for the private sector investment funds (PRIs).
- The GCF will provide FAO with a cash pool and add a cash buffer for the next ten years. Any GCF funds that have not been disbursed by the end of each year will be returned to the GCF.

### 318. External Audit.
- External audits will be conducted annually by the Internal Oversight and Integrity Office (IOIO) and the United Nations Office of Internal Oversight Services (UNOIOS). The IOIO will conduct an annual audit of the accounts managed by IFAD, in accordance with the GCF accounting and reporting guidelines.

### 319. External Audit.
- External audits will be conducted annually by the Internal Oversight and Integrity Office (IOIO) and the United Nations Office of Internal Oversight Services (UNOIOS). The IOIO will conduct an annual audit of the accounts managed by IFAD, in accordance with the GCF accounting and reporting guidelines.

Dear Ms. Duarte,

Re: Funding proposal for the GCF by International Fund for Agricultural Development (IFAD) regarding their interventions for Migration and Adaptation (DaMA)

We refer to the programme title **Daily Interventions for Migration and Adaptation (DIMMA)** which was submitted to the Green Climate Fund (GCF) on 13 September 2024. The undertaking is the daily authorized representative of IFAD National Treasury, the undersigned hereby acknowledges receipt of your funding proposal.

Pursuant to GCF Decision B.08/15, the present document shall serve as acknowledgement of having received our no-objection to the programme as included in the funding proposal.

By communicating our no-objection, it is implied that:

(1) The government of the Republic of Kenya has no objection to the programme as proposed by IFAD and the GCF;

(2) The programme is included in the funding proposals in conformity with the national priorities, strategies and plans of the Republic of Kenya.

Yours sincerely,

[Signature]

# RWANDA ENVIRONMENT MANAGEMENT AUTHORITY (REMA)

Kigali, Rwanda

Executive Director
Green Climate Fund

Date: October 20, 2023

Ref: REMA/GCF/Grant Agreement/PA0NET

Subject: No objection for the GCF by Rwanda Environment Management Authority regarding PA0NET

We are pleased to inform you that Rwanda has no objection to the funding proposal submitted by International Fund for Agricultural Development (IFAD) on behalf of Rwanda Environment Management Authority, the Implementing Entity of Rwanda.

Pursuant to GCF decision 9/15, the content of which we acknowledge to have received, we hereby provide our no objection to the funding proposal.

By submitting this no-objection, it is implied that:

- The government of Rwanda has no objections to the programme as included in the funding proposal.
- The programme as included in the funding proposal is in conformity with the national priorities, objectives and plans of Rwanda.
- The programme is aligned with the environmental and social safeguards. The programme as included in the funding proposal is in conformity with relevant national laws and regulations.

We acknowledge that our no-objection process for providing no objections to the programme does not constitute an endorsement by REMA or any other entity of the programme, its contents, or the implementing entity. We also wish to clarify that our no objection does not imply that we have reviewed the programme in detail.

We acknowledge that it is a matter of responsibility of the REMA/GCF to ensure that the programme is implemented in accordance with the GCF policies and procedures.

Yours sincerely,

John Karemera
Director General

Rwanda Environment Management Authority (REMA)

# Secretariat’s assessment of FP209

**Proposal title:** Dirty Interventions for Mitigation and Adaptation (DiMA)

**Accredited entity:** International Fund for Agricultural Development (IFAD)

**Countries:** Kenya, Rwanda, Uganda and United Republic of Tanzania

**Proposed/programme size:** Large

## I. Overall assessment of the Secretariat

### 1. Strengths
- The **Great Green Wall** initiative brings together multi-country programming, which could lead to comprehensive implementation across countries.
- The programme has strong potential for increasing synergies and reducing transaction costs through regional coordination and harmonization.
- Secretariat programme designed to hold multiple countries accountable for achieving results across the Great Green Wall through implementing country projects.

### 2. Weaknesses
- The Board may wish to consider approving this funding proposal in accordance with the conditions set out in paragraph 10 of decision 9347/EM.IFD, which states that the programme should be subject to the conditions set out in annex II to decision 9347/EM.IFD.

## II. Summary of the Secretariat’s assessment

### 2.1 Programme background
- The **Great Green Wall** is located in Africa, including Benin, Burkina Faso, Chad, Ethiopia, Mali, Niger, Nigeria, Senegal and Sudan.
- The programme aims to restore degraded land and promote sustainable management of natural resources through high-level political engagement at the highest levels of government.
- The programme has strong potential for increasing synergies and reducing transaction costs through regional coordination and harmonization.

### 2.2 Programme design
- The programme is designed to support integrated approaches of climate-smart and low-carbon practices and technologies.

# To address the often-mentioned challenges, this programme, “THMAA: Daily Interaction for Migration and Adaptation”, will have a comprehensive and holistic approach to enhance the livelihoods of dairy farmers and their families.

## Objectives

- Promote active environments by boosting the capacity of smallholder farmers, including women, youth, and elderly people.
- Encourage the adoption of modern technologies in dairy farming.
- Improve the quality of life for dairy farmers through better access to markets and improved income levels.
- Enhance the livelihoods of dairy farmers and their families.

## Results

The total budget for the programme is USD 108.26 million. THMAA will be implemented over a period of five years, from 2023 to 2027. The programme will focus on three main components:

- **Component 1: Capacity Development** (USD 45.29 million)
  - This component aims to enhance the capacity of dairy farmers and their families.
  - It includes training programmes, workshops, and other activities aimed at improving the skills and knowledge of dairy farmers.

- **Component 2: Market Access and Value Chain Development** (USD 31.87 million)
  - This component focuses on enhancing market access for dairy farmers.
  - It includes activities such as improving infrastructure, establishing market linkages, and promoting value-added products.

- **Component 3: Policy and Institutional Support** (USD 30.94 million)
  - This component aims to support policy reforms and institutional strengthening in the dairy sector.
  - It includes activities such as policy advocacy, institutional capacity building, and research and development.

## Implementation Strategy

The programme will be implemented through a combination of direct interventions and indirect benefits. The specific activities under each component are outlined below:

### Component 1: Capacity Development

- Training programmes for farmers on modern dairy farming techniques.
- Establishment of demonstration farms to showcase best practices.
- Provision of credit and other financial services to farmers.

### Component 2: Market Access and Value Chain Development

- Construction of collection centers and milk chilling facilities.
- Establishment of market information systems to improve market access.
- Promotion of value-added products such as cheese, yogurt, and ice cream.

### Component 3: Policy and Institutional Support

- Advocacy for policy reforms to support the dairy sector.
- Strengthening of institutions responsible for dairy development.
- Conducting research on dairy farming practices and technologies.

## Target Groups

The programme will target the following groups:

- Smallholder dairy farmers (Target 1)
- Women and youth in dairy farming (Target 2)
- Dairy cooperatives and associations (Target 3)

## Specific Objectives

The specific objectives of the programme are as follows:

- Increase milk production by 15% through improved feeding practices.
- Enhance the income of smallholder farmers by 20% through better market access.
- Improve the nutritional status of children under five by promoting consumption of dairy products.

## Implementation Plan

The programme will be implemented over a period of five years, from 2023 to 2027. The implementation plan includes the following activities:

### Year 1: Capacity Building and Preparation

- Conduct baseline surveys.
- Develop training materials.
- Establish demonstration farms.

### Year 2: Implementation of Training Programmes

- Conduct training programmes for farmers.
- Establish collection centers.
- Promote value-added products.

### Years 3 to 5: Consolidation and Expansion

- Expand the programme to new areas.
- Strengthen market linkages.
- Conduct policy advocacy.

## Conclusion

The THMAA programme aims to enhance the livelihoods of dairy farmers by promoting active environments, improving market access, and supporting policy reforms. Through a comprehensive approach, the programme will address the challenges faced by dairy farmers and contribute to the overall development of the dairy sector.

# Green Climate Fund

## Activity 1.1: Improving GCF Emission Management System

Businesses are developing national MRV systems to track diary emissions accurately and sustain climate commitments. Activity 1.1 will improve GCF emission management systems by collecting data on greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions from dairy farms, including enteric fermentation, manure management, feed production, and soil carbon sequestration. This activity will enable businesses to reduce climate risk and vulnerability assessment, strengthen early warning systems, and enhance climate resilience. It will also support the development of multiple daily statistics for accurate carbon footprinting by applying verification methods and strengthening data reporting.

Activity 1.1 will focus on improving knowledge management and digital cooperation to promote the adoption of best practices in MRV systems. This activity will support the development of a comprehensive database that includes regional reports, research studies, and best practices. Activity 1.4 will establish regional networks for knowledge sharing and collaboration among stakeholders, researchers, extensionists, and other relevant actors. These platforms will facilitate the exchange of information on climate-smart agriculture and sustainable farming practices.

### Output 2.1: Enhancing systems and products for multi-holder dairy farms

- Component 2 focuses on enhancing systems and products for multi-holder dairy farms.
- This component aims to improve the sustainability, climate resilience, and economic growth of smallholder dairy farmers.
- The activity will develop a comprehensive system that includes data collection, analysis, and reporting tools to support sustainable farming practices.

### Output 3.1: (Diffusion/Adoption and capacity development strategies)

- Output 3.1 aims to enhance the adoption of climate-smart agriculture by smallholder dairy farmers.
- Activity 2.1 will enhance governance capacity and develop regional networks for knowledge sharing among stakeholders, researchers, extensionists, and other relevant actors.
- This activity will promote the adoption of best practices in MRV systems and support the development of regional networks.

### Output 4.1: (Building, actual health, and safety management expertise)

- Output 4.1 aims to build capacity for climate-smart agriculture by enhancing the skills and knowledge of farmers, extensionists, and other relevant actors.
- This activity will focus on developing a comprehensive system that includes data collection, analysis, and reporting tools to support sustainable farming practices.

# Objectives

## Objective 2: (Farmer and grasslands management) improved aims to increase pasture and grassland management to enhance soil resilience, biodiversity, and carbon sequestration.

- Improved grazing management will be achieved by supporting community-based pasture management planning, setting land tenure rights, and providing incentives for sustainable grazing practices.
- Farmers will be supported through training in rangeland health status monitoring, grazing planning, and forage budgeting.
- Farmers will be supported to increase soil organic carbon (SOC) sequestration through improved water availability to dry areas by investing in water harvesting systems, such as small-scale irrigation, rainwater harvesting, and soil moisture conservation techniques.
- These interventions will support sustainable land protection.

# Green Climate Fund

## Project Title: International Livestock Research Institute (ILRI)

### Objectives and Outcomes
- Promote greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions reductions through improved livestock management.
- Enhance food security and livelihoods of smallholder farmers in sub-Saharan Africa.

### Activities and Results
- Support national governments to develop policies and strategies for livestock sector development.
- Improve the productivity, efficiency, and resilience of smallholder livestock systems.
- Reduce GHG emissions from livestock production through improved management practices.

### Project Management Unit
- ILRI will implement the project with support from various partners including FAO, ICARDA, and others.
- The program will operate in Ethiopia, Kenya, Tanzania, Uganda, and the United Republic of Tanzania.
- Regional offices in Nairobi and Addis Ababa will coordinate activities with national and regional livestock officers.

### Assessment of Performance against Investment Criteria

#### 3.1 Impact Potential
- Scale: High
- The project aims to benefit over 15 million smallholder farmers across sub-Saharan Africa.
- The program contributes to the achievement of multiple SDGs, including SDG 2 (Zero Hunger), SDG 8 (Decent Work and Economic Growth), and SDG 13 (Climate Action).
- The program is expected to reduce GHG emissions by 50% through improved livestock management practices.
- ILRI will work with national governments to ensure the sustainability of project outcomes.

#### 3.2 Paradigm Shift Potential
- Scale: High
- The project takes a holistic approach to reducing the dairy sector's carbon footprint in East Africa, aiming to increase the adoption of sustainable dairy production practices.

# Strengthening key frameworks

Institutional capacity and service delivery. A sustainable reporting framework across climate-related sectors to unearth business risks. It seeks to drive a more integrated approach to climate change by enhancing sector-specific and financial mechanisms in prominent institutions, leveraging existing structures that are already engaged with businesses. This includes the integration of climate risk management into core operations, alongside improved regulatory frameworks and the presentation of climate-related disclosures.

## a.
The programme enhances access to finance and alternative technologies, creating an enabling environment for sustainable development. It seeks to ensure financial and technological solutions that have traditionally excluded large parts of society are made more accessible. This includes enhancing access to affordable renewable energy with low carbon footprints, thereby reducing the carbon intensity of economies. Additionally, it supports the adoption of climate-smart practices and technologies in agriculture, forestry, fisheries, and other sectors. It also promotes integrated approaches that contribute to a low-carbon, climate-resilient future while addressing the needs of vulnerable populations.

## b.
Additionally, it lays down groundwork for long-term structural transformation, ensuring that the benefits are widely shared by all stakeholders. This includes strengthening market systems and promoting sustainable consumption and production patterns. It also seeks to enhance the resilience of communities and ecosystems through integrated approaches to climate change adaptation and mitigation.

# 3.3 Sustainable development potential

## a.
Bhutan adheres to several Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) by enhancing resource efficiency, improving food security and nutrition, fostering sustainable cities and communities, promoting responsible consumption and production, and supporting biodiversity conservation and sustainable use of natural resources.

It supports the transition towards low-carbon economies through renewable energy adoption. It also promotes high-quality education and health services, ensuring universal access to basic services. Strengthening water management systems is crucial for sustainable development. Additionally, it supports better food security and nutrition outcomes by promoting sustainable agriculture practices that enhance soil fertility and improve ecosystem resilience.

## b.
It also supports environmental sustainability by enhancing and conserving natural resources, including forests, wetlands, and biodiversity. It promotes the use of renewable energy sources to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. It also seeks to enhance climate resilience through integrated approaches to disaster risk reduction and climate change adaptation. This includes promoting sustainable urban development and improving access to safe water and sanitation.

# 3.4 Needs of the recipient

## Scale: Medium to high


---
**Page 121**
---

# The sustainability of dairy farming in the region is increasingly at risk among rising temperatures, erratic weather patterns and resource constraints, all of which negatively affect pasture quality and animal productivity. Climate change impacts on the availability of water resources, are particularly vulnerable as they operate on a small-scale with limited access to credit, technology and markets. The sector also faces challenges from unsustainable farming practices, inadequate access to quality feed, veterinary services and climate-smart technologies. Additionally, limited access to markets and poor infrastructure hinder farmers’ ability to adopt sustainable dairy farming methods. Financial barriers further hinder investments in improved breeding stock and better management practices. The lack of access to credit and insurance also prevents farmers from investing in improved breeds and better management of their production. In response farmers with less resources are increasingly adopting intensive systems, which reduce the resilience and sustainability of the dairy sector in that area.

## 3.5 Country oversight

### Scale: High

GdALA uses a comprehensive framework to address the impacts of climate change on the dairy sector by providing guidance for adaptation strategies. The framework includes appropriate interventions, national adaptation plans for agriculture and related sectors, and the integration of climate-smart practices into national agricultural policies and technologies. The programme also provides support for the development of climate-smart value chains by promoting climate-resilient practices and technologies, the programme aims to enhance the resilience of dairy farmers to climate change.

# SEAI mitigation measures have been included, including post mitigation, to prove that any residual SEAI/GHG emissions are addressed and monitored accordingly. ## 4.2 Gender policy

In line with the GEF Gender Policy, the AB should endorse a policy statement that recognizes the importance of gender equality and equal opportunities (GE/SEI) action plans, including gender mainstreaming and specific actions to this project. The gender assessment should be the basis for the development of an action plan. The gender action plan will be carried out through an implementation period of 4 years. The GEF Gender Policy will also be the basis for the development of a gender action plan. The AB should endorse the gender action plan and include it in the project document. The specific technical assistance will include (i) capacity building to ensure that the project is implemented with a gender perspective, (ii) the development of a gender action plan, (iii) the recruitment of experienced service providers to support the implementation of the GEF Gender Policy, and (iv) the preparation of a gender action plan.

### Specific actions

- Include 10% of the total budget allocated to the project for gender-related activities.
- Include 50% of the technical assistance in the project for gender-related activities.
- Include 30% of the staff in the project for gender-related activities.

## 4.3 Risks

### Overall project/programme assessment (high risk)

#### 4.3.1 Accreditation entity/executing entity capacity to execute the current project/programme (low risk)

- The executing entity has a strong track record in implementing projects, including those related to renewable energy.

### Project/programme-specific execution risks (high risk)

#### 4.3.2 Severe credit risk
- The GEF loan will be extended to the government of four host countries with different levels of creditworthiness.
- The executing entity has a strong track record in implementing projects, including those related to renewable energy.

## 4.4 Fidelity

IPA serves as the IE and is responsible for overall programme management, including approval, finance and technical authority, supervision and control in line with its terms of reference. The IPA will be supported by the National Audit Office (NAO) to ensure that the programme is delivered on time, within budget and to the required quality standards. The NAO will provide independent assurance to the programme board, including assurance on the programme's governance arrangements, risk management and financial control. The programme will also be subject to regular scrutiny by the Public Accounts Committee.

Governance: I will continue to recommend GCP, reporting directly to the Secretary of State for Environment, Food and Rural Affairs (Defra). There will be an open and transparent relationship between the programme board and IPA. There will be a clear distinction between the programme management team and the delivery teams. The programme board will have oversight of the programme's strategic direction and performance.

# The program contributes to three GEF fresh water areas: infiltration through forests and land use adaptation through the amount of most vulnerable people and communities, and biodiversity conservation. The program will contribute to the GEF strategic objective of reducing emissions (both avoided and removed) associated with an expanded definition in emissions reductions, including those from agriculture, forestry, and other land uses. The program will reduce emissions by 15 million tons of CO2 equivalent annually over a period of five years, with an overall reduction of 75 million tons of CO2 equivalent over the life of the program. These indicators are consistent with the GEF strategic objective of reducing emissions from deforestation and forest degradation.

# Therefore it is recommended that the Board considers whether Disclosures of GCF proceeds should ideally be made after GCF has obtained satisfactory protection against legal and reputational risks.

## The Board may wish to consider the following issues in relation to this recommendation:

- Whether the GCF funds, the disbursed funds, or both should enter a financing arrangement with the host country, using through its own resources or those of an independent approved entity;
- Whether the GCF funds are earmarked for the programme, under the Accredited Entity’s reported target and budget;
- Whether the GCF funds are earmarked for the programme, under the Accredited Entity’s reported target and budget, which would make it reasonable for the financing arrangement to be made by the Accredited Entity on behalf of the GCF;
- Whether the MA and/or other entities “TA” on such wholesale basis have the adequate safeguards in place to ensure that the disbursements are made in accordance with the requirements and deliverables set out in the project proposal, and deliverables not in conflict with the objectives of the MA and would entail acting in breach of fiduciary duties;
- Whether the MA and/or other entities “TA” on such wholesale basis have the adequate safeguards in place to ensure that the disbursements are made in accordance with the requirements and deliverables set out in the project proposal, and deliverables not in conflict with the objectives of the MA and would entail acting in breach of fiduciary duties;
- Whether the MA and/or other entities “TA” on such wholesale basis have the adequate safeguards in place to ensure that the disbursements are made in accordance with the requirements and deliverables set out in the project proposal, and deliverables not in conflict with the objectives of the MA and would entail acting in breach of fiduciary duties.

## From the Board’s perspective, it is important to consider whether the safeguards in place for the GCF funds are adequate to ensure that the disbursements are made in accordance with the requirements and deliverables set out in the project proposal, and deliverables not in conflict with the objectives of the MA and would entail acting in breach of fiduciary duties.

## To conclude the matters raised in this section, it is recommended that agreed by the Board:

- Submissions by the Accredited Entity to the Fund for a certificate or legal opinion, in forms acceptable to the GCF Secretariat, confirming that the Accredited Entity has obtained all local necessary approvals needed by law and regulations;
- Signatures of the FAO, a firm and independent satisfactory to the GCF Secretariat within 180 days from the date of such approval, at the latest the Accredited Entity’s legal opinion confirming that it has obtained all local necessary approvals needed by law and regulations;
- Signatures of the FAO, a firm and independent satisfactory to the GCF Secretariat within 180 days from the date of such approval, at the latest the Accredited Entity’s legal opinion confirming that it has obtained all local necessary approvals needed by law and regulations;
- Signatures of the FAO, a firm and independent satisfactory to the GCF Secretariat within 180 days from the date of such approval, at the latest the Accredited Entity’s legal opinion confirming that it has obtained all local necessary approvals needed by law and regulations.

# Independent Technical Advisory Panel’s review of FP269

**Proposed name:** Daily Interventions to Mitigate and Adapt (DIMA)

**Accepted entity:** International Fund for Agricultural Development (IFAD)

**Countries:** Kenya, Rwanda, Uganda and three Replenished Regions of Tanzania

**Program/participant size:** Large

## I. Assessment of the Independent Technical Advisory Panel

1. The funding proposal is for a large, public-sector programmatic intervention in environmental and social safeguards (ESG) implemented by the International Fund for Agricultural Development (IFAD). The program will operate over six years in four countries: Kenya, Rwanda, Uganda, and three Replenished Regions of Tanzania.

2. The total cost of the programme is USD 35.82 million, of which USD 10 million is provided by IFAD and USD 25.82 million is requested from other sources (see section IV: Budget).

3. The ESG for the proposed intervention is based on the National Safeguard Policies of the Government of Kenya, the Government of Rwanda through its National Strategy, the Ministry of Water and Environment of Uganda, and the Environmental Management Act of Tanzania. The programme will be implemented in line with IFAD’s ESG policy and procedures.

4. The area of the proposed programme is:

   - **Kenya:** 10 counties
   - **Rwanda:** 3 districts
   - **Uganda:** 2 districts
   - **Tanzania:** 3 regions

5. The aim of the proposed programme is to increase the resilience of targeted, dependent communities by increasing their access to and control over natural resources while improving the resilience of their ecosystems.

6. The programme will support climate-smart interventions that enhance ecosystem services, improve livelihoods, and strengthen community-based resource management systems. It will also promote gender equality and social inclusion in line with IFAD’s ESG policy.

7. The programme’s key objective is to increase the resilience of targeted communities by increasing their access to and control over natural resources while improving the resilience of their ecosystems.

8. The programme’s overall change is to contribute to improved livelihoods, well-being, and food security for vulnerable rural households through sustainable management of natural resources and ecosystem services.

7. This assessment is based on the draft and final funding proposal package submitted by IFAD on 26 March 2023.

# Green Climate Fund

## Integrated approaches and well-managed landscapes to increase productivity, reduce emissions intensity, and decrease absolute emissions compared to business-as-usual scenarios

### (i) Farm and land-use practices to increase productivity, reduce emissions intensity, and decrease absolute emissions compared to business-as-usual scenarios; and (ii) landscape-level approaches that include carbon sequestration, and water and habitat management for natural forests and other ecosystems to increase the net removal of greenhouse gases from the atmosphere

## The programme structure

The programme structure is under three components, as shown in Table 1.

### Table 1. Structure of the four interventions in Mitigation and Adaptation programme

**Component 1: Integrated national capacity and enhanced knowledge management for low emissions climate resilient dairy sector**

- **Output 1.1:** Climate change policy and regulatory capacities of the targeted sectors are strengthened
- **Output 1.2:** National and sub-national policies driving the dairy sector towards climate resilience are developed
- **Output 1.3:** Integrated capacity building, knowledge management and monitoring of climate change impacts on dairy value chains

**Component 2: Enhancing low-emission and climate-resilient dairy value chain development**

- **Output 2.1:** Smallholder dairy farmers and processors technical and financial capacities are enhanced
- **Output 2.2:** Breeding and animal health feed and fodder, and water management practices are adopted
