# Green Economy Coalition

## Comment J: Green Urban Infrastructure Facility

### Outcome A: Essential infrastructure made available to support green urban infrastructure investments to build the necessary enabling environment for sustainable cities and communities.

#### Impact potential

The programme will target two of the GCF results areas for adaptation, aiming to:

- Increase resilience to climate change impacts in urban areas (LARA.1.1) and disaster risk reduction (DRR) outcomes (DRRA1). Despite the core of this programme being focused on adaptation, it is recognised that the area of increased resilience to climate change will also have benefits for mitigation.
- Increase resilience to climate change through enhanced urban infrastructure investments in cities (LARA.1.2).

In addition, the programme will target two of the GCF results areas for mitigation:

- Increase access to renewable energy and increase energy efficiency (MRE.1).
- Increase access to sustainable transport systems (MRE.3).

### Climate change risks and impacts

This funding proposal highlights the climate change-related risks and impacts that facing urban communities, particularly in low-income countries. These include:

- **Increased frequency of extreme weather events**: This has led to an increase in the number of people affected by floods, droughts, and other natural disasters.
- **Increased vulnerability**: Urban areas are increasingly vulnerable to climate change due to their high population density and lack of green spaces.
- **Reduced access to basic services**: Climate change can lead to reduced access to clean water and sanitation, as well as increased air pollution.

The programme aims to address these risks by:

- Improving the resilience of urban infrastructure to extreme weather events.
- Increasing access to renewable energy sources for urban communities.
- Promoting sustainable transport systems in cities.

### Scale: Medium to high

The programme is expected to have a significant impact on the lives of millions of people living in urban areas. It will contribute to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), particularly SDG 11 (sustainable cities and communities) and SDG 7 (affordable and clean energy).

The programme will also support the implementation of the Paris Agreement by increasing access to renewable energy and promoting sustainable transport systems.

# Green Climate Fund

## Mitigation impact potential: The GCMA programmatic rates to achieve total mitigation outcomes from a combination of emission reduction (from the introduction of renewable energy) and avoidance of emissions (from the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions)

### Overview

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a multilateral fund established by the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) in 2010 to support mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. The GCMA programmatic rates are designed to achieve total mitigation outcomes from a combination of emission reduction and avoidance of emissions.

### Mitigation impact potential

The GCMA programmatic rates aim to achieve total mitigation outcomes from a combination of emission reduction (from the introduction of renewable energy) and avoidance of emissions (from the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions).

### Table: Main programme interventions and how they contribute to mitigation results (referred to in the following sections)

| Intervention type | Type of intervention | Time frame | Summary of how new mitigation results are the outcome |
|------------------|----------------------|------------|--------------------------------------------------------|
| Improved energy efficiency | Energy efficiency reduction | 10 years | Improved energy efficiency contributes to reducing greenhouse gas emissions by increasing energy efficiency in buildings, industry, and transport. |

### Notes

1. Mitigation programmatic rates from 2015 with 830 million USD approved (1 April 2015).
2. Based on the latest available data as of October 2023.

# Green Climate Fund

## Performance Calendar

The performance calendar provides an overview of scheduled activities and collective results table for upcoming events. It is designed to provide a comprehensive view of the GCF's performance against its strategic objectives. The calendar includes key milestones such as:

- Monitoring and evaluation market readiness assessments.
- Enhanced transparency framework implementation.
- Accreditation of new entities.
- Implementation of the results-based climate finance approach.

### Key Objectives

1. **Enhanced Transparency Framework**: The enhanced transparency framework (ETF) aims to improve the quality, consistency, and comparability of information on climate actions. This includes:

   - Reporting on greenhouse gas emissions by activities.
   - Tracking progress towards adaptation and mitigation goals.
   - Enhancing the effectiveness of climate action.

2. **Monitoring, Reporting, and Verification (MRV)**: The MRV system is designed to monitor, report, and verify the impacts of GCF-funded projects. This includes:

   - Establishing a robust MRV system for accredited entities.
   - Ensuring consistency with the Paris Agreement's requirements.
   - Enhancing the quality and reliability of reported data.

3. **Accreditation**: The accreditation process ensures that entities meet the standards set by the GCF. This includes:

   - Evaluating the capacity of entities to implement projects.
   - Ensuring compliance with environmental and social safeguards.
   - Facilitating access to funding for accredited entities.

### Paradigm Shift Potential

Paradigms shift relates to the degree to which the proposed programs can successfully address the challenges posed by climate change. The potential for a paradigm shift is assessed as follows:

- **Medium**: The GCF's activities are expected to contribute significantly to addressing climate change, with opportunities for enhanced impact through improved implementation and scaling.
- **High**: There is significant potential for transformative changes in areas such as adaptation, mitigation, and capacity building. This includes the development of new technologies, policies, and practices that can lead to long-term sustainability.

Overall, the impact potential is rated as medium to high.

### Social benefits

# e-waste(1) through the repair of the installed renewable energy based equipment and the establishment of a repairment maintenance system as a lead financial institution for each

## 2. Knowledge co-creation
This programme is expected to generate adequate positive externalities through strengthening some growing capacities, and capacity building activities. The programme will have access to essential technical services to improve protection procedures. The approach will be based on the development of a comprehensive framework that includes the following: (i) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system; (ii) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system; and (iii) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system.

## 3. Seed money, pastoralists
Seed money is provided to the programme focus on intensive and extensive activities that are related to the climate change interventions, especially grazing, watering, and pasture management. The seed money will be used to support the pastoralist communities in the areas of: (i) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system; (ii) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system; and (iii) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system.

## 4. Seed money, women
Seed money is provided to the programme as a condition from the CAF for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system that will be used to support the pastoralist communities in the areas of: (i) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system; (ii) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system; and (iii) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system.

## 5. Needs of the recipient
The needs of the recipient are classified as being highly vulnerable to climate change, observed and projected temperature patterns, as well as hot spells, increased frequency and severity of droughts and floods, and changes in precipitation patterns. The programme will be used to support the pastoralist communities in the areas of: (i) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system; (ii) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system; and (iii) capacity building for the establishment of a repairment maintenance system.

# Alignment with policies

The EIMA programme aligns with the national strategies of the preceding decade to support and commissioning existing plans, policies and programmes. It also aligns with the approved sector Development Strategy (2019-2029), National Agricultural Policy (2018-2030) and National Agricultural Investment Plan (NAIP) 2019-2023. The programme aligns with the following:

- **National Climate Change and Green Growth Strategy** (2015-2025)
- **National Climate Change Policy**
- **National Climate Change Programme**
- **National Climate Change Investment Plan**
- **National Adaptation Plan**
- **National Mitigation Plan**

The leading programme is aligned with the National Development Strategy 1 (NDS1) and the National Long-term Development Plan 3 (NDLP3). The programme aligns with the following priority areas:

- **Priority Area 2**: the leading programme Benefit Sharing Mechanism
- **Priority Area 5**: the leading programme Benefit Sharing Mechanism

## Engagement with stakeholders

The stakeholder engagement plan focuses on the following key groups: national and local government, private sector, civil society organisations (CSOs), academia, media, youth and women. The engagement will be conducted through:

- **National Policy Dialogues** (NPDs) in 2021 and 2024
- **Regional Consultations** with the four largest countries were diversity and inclusion considerations are prioritised

The NPDs will be held in April 2021, September 2023 and February 2024. The regional consultations will be held in July-September 2021 through regional dialogues. Through regional dialogues, the programme will engage with a wide range of stakeholders including national and regional public institutions, private sector, CSOs, academia, media, youth and women. - **National Policy Dialogues** (NPDs) in 2021 and 2024

# Green Belt Movement

## Efficiency and effectiveness

### With a total budget for the proposed GBM programme of USD 158,900, additional (PA) is required to implement the programme. A2 are cost of the overall budget (PA) is estimated at 42 per cent of the total budget. The PA is investing in the following areas: (i) establishment and maintenance of tree nurseries; (ii) planting of trees on farms and gardens, and (iii) establishment of tree-based farm forestry enterprises.

### The programme will be implemented over a period of two years, from January 1992 to December 1993. In the first year, the programme will focus on establishing tree nurseries and planting trees on farms and gardens. In the second year, the programme will focus on establishing tree-based farm forestry enterprises.

### The programme will be implemented by the Green Belt Movement (GBM), a non-governmental organization that has been working in Kenya for over 20 years. The GBM has extensive experience in implementing environmental programmes and has a strong track record of success.

### The programme will be implemented in partnership with local communities, government agencies, and other organizations. The GBM will provide technical assistance and training to local communities, and will work with government agencies to ensure that the programme is consistent with national policies and strategies.

### The programme will be monitored and evaluated by an independent evaluation team. The evaluation team will assess the effectiveness of the programme in achieving its objectives, and will make recommendations for improving the programme.

### Local farmers: In addition to the technical assistance provided by GBM, local farmers will receive training in tree planting and maintenance techniques. The programme will also provide financial support to local farmers to purchase trees and other materials needed for tree planting.

### The Ministry of Agriculture (MoA) will provide technical assistance to the programme. The ministry will also provide land for establishing tree nurseries and will work with GBM to ensure that the programme is consistent with national policies and strategies.

### The programme will be implemented in partnership with local communities, government agencies, and other organizations. The GBM will provide technical assistance and training to local communities, and will work with government agencies to ensure that the programme is consistent with national policies and strategies.

### The programme will be monitored and evaluated by an independent evaluation team. The evaluation team will assess the effectiveness of the programme in achieving its objectives, and will make recommendations for improving the programme.

## Introduction

The programme flow includes a complex set of leading indicator activities for the range of GCF’s focus areas. These include:

1. Direct support to farmers.
2. Direct support to households as beneficiaries.
3. IPA (International Fund for Agricultural Development) and other IFIs (International Financial Institutions) activities that are directly linked to the programme.

The programme flow also includes a set of monitoring, reporting, and verification (MRV) activities that are designed to ensure the integrity of the programme’s results. These include:

1. Monitoring of the programme’s performance.
2. Reporting on the programme’s progress.
3. Verification of the programme’s results.

### Economic analysis

The ex-ante financial and economic viability of the programme is demonstrated by an assessment and financial analysis conducted by the AF (Agricultural Fund) (note 3 in the table below). The programme’s financial viability is assessed as a whole, at aggregated levels. The aggregated financial viability of the programme for agriculture as a whole is presented in Table 1. The total cost of the programme is USD 270 million, with an estimated benefit-cost ratio (BCR) of 3.56. This indicates that the programme’s benefits exceed its costs by approximately 2.56 times. The programme’s financial viability is also assessed at the level of individual activities. Table 2 presents the results of this assessment for each activity. The highest BCR is achieved by the activity “Promotion of commercial farming” (BCR = 17.8), followed by the activity “Promotion of smallholder farming” (BCR = 6.5). The lowest BCR is achieved by the activity “Promotion of agro-processing” (BCR = 0.9).

# INTRODUCTION

The G8 countries and several include (G8+) governments and other parties plan for implementation of biotech interventions in Africa, Kenya, Uganda, and Nigeria. Part 1 provides a contextual overview of the socio-economic, political, and environmental factors that shape agricultural development in these countries. It also highlights the importance of biotechnology as an enabling technology to address food security challenges. The G8+ countries have committed to support actions to achieve the Millennium Development Goals (MDGs) through increased investments in agriculture and rural development. These actions include the use of biotech crops, which can be further enhanced by the adoption of other technologies such as integrated pest management (IPM), crop rotation, and sustainable farming practices.

The G8+ countries have a clear vision to increase agricultural productivity and food security. The G8+ governments are committed to support the development of biotech crops in Africa, Kenya, Uganda, and Nigeria. This commitment is based on the recognition that biotechnology can be used to improve crop yields, reduce postharvest losses, and enhance the nutritional value of crops. The G8+ countries have also committed to support the development of biotech crops in other parts of the world.

The G8+ countries have a clear vision to increase agricultural productivity and food security. The G8+ governments are committed to support the development of biotech crops in Africa, Kenya, Uganda, and Nigeria. This commitment is based on the recognition that biotechnology can be used to improve crop yields, reduce postharvest losses, and enhance the nutritional value of crops. The G8+ countries have also committed to support the development of biotech crops in other parts of the world.

# PART 1: SIGNIFICANCE

Agriculture is a significant contributor to the economies of many developing countries, providing employment and income to millions of people. As a result, it has been identified as one of the key sectors that can contribute to poverty reduction and food security. According to the United Nations, the world's most impoverished regions are those where agriculture is the main source of livelihood. In these regions, agricultural productivity is often low due to lack of access to modern technologies and inputs.

Among the challenges experienced by women in business include lack of capital, inadequate training and skills, and limited access to information and markets. Women's empowerment is critical for sustainable development. Empowered women can contribute to economic growth and poverty reduction. When women are empowered, they can make decisions about their own lives and the lives of their families. This includes decisions about their reproductive health, education, and employment. Women who are empowered are more likely to be healthy, educated, and productive.

Women's empowerment is also important for the development of sustainable communities. When women are empowered, they can participate in community decision-making processes and contribute to the development of sustainable communities. Women can also play a key role in promoting gender equality and reducing discrimination against women and girls.

Women's empowerment is a complex issue that requires a multi-sectoral approach. It involves addressing the root causes of gender inequality, such as poverty, lack of education, and limited access to resources. It also involves creating an enabling environment for women to participate fully in society and the economy.

Women have a right to own land according to law; however SOCIOCULTURAL practices favor male inheritance. Only 15% of women own land, compared with 40% of men. Women's access to land is often limited by cultural norms that restrict their ability to inherit or own land. These norms are reinforced by traditional practices such as patrilineality and patrilocality.

Youth unemployment. Youth in East Africa accounts for over 60 percent of those between the ages of 15-35. Youth unemployment is a major challenge in sub-Saharan Africa and has been exacerbated by the COVID-19 pandemic. The youth are increasingly engaged in sub-optimal employment (self-employment) in subsistence agriculture and family-based livelihood activities. This employment is characterized by low productivity, high vulnerability to shocks, and limited access to credit and financial services.

As the largest employer in the country, the agricultural sector remains a key entry point for job creation, business opportunities, and economic growth. The youth are increasingly engaged in productive enterprises in every sector that bring about social and economic development. Investing in youth is critical as it provides a platform to tap into the potential of a young workforce. Youth employment is a major driver of economic growth and development.

It is worth noting that the youth represent 70 percent of Kenya's labor force by international standards of work. The youth are also the largest consumers of goods and services in the economy, which has a multiplier effect on the overall economy. The youth are also the largest users of ICTs, social media technologies, and mobile money transfers, which have been instrumental in driving economic growth and development.

The Government of Tanzania (GoT) is implementing the Food and Agriculture Sector Development Support Programme (FASDEP II), funded by the Ministry of Agriculture (MoA), in collaboration with the World Bank. FASDEP II aims to strengthen agricultural sector development support services, improve food security and nutrition, and enhance the livelihoods of poor farmers through improved productivity and sustainable management of agricultural resources. # Gender-Based Violence Victimization (GBV) and Mental Health Outcomes

Gender-based violence (GBV) victimization is a significant public health issue that affects individuals of all genders, ages, races, and ethnicities. It includes physical, sexual, psychological, and economic harm caused by intimate partners or non-partners. GBV has serious consequences for both individual and community health.

## Prevalence and Impact

GBV is a global problem affecting millions of people each year. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), 1 in 3 women worldwide have experienced physical or sexual violence from an intimate partner, while 1 in 7 men have been sexually assaulted by an intimate partner. GBV has profound effects on mental health, including post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD), depression, anxiety, and substance abuse.

## Gender Differences

Women are disproportionately affected by GBV compared to men. This disparity is influenced by cultural, social, and economic factors that perpetuate gender inequality. Women who experience GBV often face additional challenges such as stigma, lack of support, and limited access to healthcare services. Men who experience GBV may also face unique barriers due to societal expectations surrounding masculinity.

## Children and Adolescents

GBV affects children and adolescents as well. According to the WHO, 1 in 5 women aged 15-49 reported experiencing sexual violence during childhood. Childhood exposure to GBV can have long-lasting effects on mental health, including increased risk for depression, anxiety, and substance abuse.

## Interventions and Support

Interventions aimed at reducing the impact of GBV include psychological support, legal assistance, and community-based programs. These interventions aim to provide a safe space for victims to share their experiences and receive support. Additionally, policies that address gender inequality and promote gender equity can help reduce the prevalence of GBV.

## Policy Implications

Policies addressing GBV must consider the unique needs of different populations. For example, policies should include provisions for culturally sensitive services, economic empowerment programs, and legal protections for victims. Furthermore, policies should aim to address the root causes of GBV, such as gender inequality and poverty.

## Conclusion

GBV is a serious public health issue that affects individuals of all genders, ages, and backgrounds. Addressing GBV requires a multi-faceted approach that includes prevention, intervention, and support services. By implementing comprehensive policies and programs, communities can work to reduce the prevalence of GBV and improve mental health outcomes for victims.

## References

- World Health Organization. (2013). Global and regional estimates of violence against women: Prevalence of intimate partner violence. Retrieved from [WHO website](https://www.who.int/reproductivehealth/topics/violence/prevalence/IPVprevalence/en/)
- Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. (2019). Intimate Partner Violence. Retrieved from [CDC website](https://www.cdc.gov/violenceprevention/intimatepartnerviolence/index.html)

# The target beneficiaries for DEMAND will include:

- People with disabilities and their families, including persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities, and their carers.
- People with disabilities, including women, children and seniors, grown such as persons with disabilities and elderly people in rural settings.

Others who will benefit will include users of inclusive early learning services and facilities.

## Targeting strategies

The targeting strategy will be to identify and engage with key stakeholders, including:

- The Ministry of Education (MoE) and the National Council for Special Educational Needs (NCSEN).
- The public sector, such as schools and education offices.
- Non-governmental organizations (NGOs), disability advocacy groups, and other relevant bodies.

## Targeting strategies and approaches

The targeting strategy will consist of:

1. Geographic targeting, to identify participating districts and regions and sites in Uganda, Kenya, Tanzania and Rwanda, where daily life has been disrupted and there is potential to develop the intervention.
2. Demographic targeting, to identify specific groups that are most affected by the intervention (e.g., children with disabilities).
3. Sectoral targeting, to identify specific sectors that will benefit from the intervention (e.g., education, health, social protection) and to complement with other interventions focusing on these areas.

The strategy will be implemented in collaboration with local partners, including:

- The Ministry of Education (MoE), which has 40 plus and 50 plus centers providing services for persons with disabilities.
- The National Council for Special Educational Needs (NCSEN).
- The Ministry of Health (MoH) and the Ministry of Gender, Labour and Social Development (MGLSD).

## PART 3: GENERAL SOCIAL INCLUSION ACTION PLAN

Demand will target 5 per cent women, 20 per cent youth, and 10 per cent children with disabilities. The action plan will be implemented in collaboration with local partners, including:

- The Ministry of Education (MoE), which has 40 plus and 50 plus centers providing services for persons with disabilities.
- The National Council for Special Educational Needs (NCSEN).
- The Ministry of Health (MoH) and the Ministry of Gender, Labour and Social Development (MGLSD).

The objective of this Duke GES action plan is to ensure that women, youth, people with disabilities, and marginalized groups are included in all social inclusion activities. This will be achieved through:

- Ensuring access to education, employment opportunities, and social services.
- Promoting the rights of persons with disabilities and advocating for their inclusion in society.

The action plan will also include specific strategies to address the needs of women, youth, people with disabilities, and marginalized groups, such as those in areas of GBV and SEAR when a direct link to not be addressed from other existing programs.

# Purpose

The purpose of the GASB Program is to increase meaningful day production and associated cost savings, revenue, and reduce their production costs through improved efficiency and other GASI measures by adopting the GASI emission reduction technologies and financial services to efficiently utilize production and revenue.

## Objectives of the Programme

To strengthen systems and institutional capacities of the western sector to enable smokeless day production and a healthy work environment that allows for increased DMR and other GASI emissions.

### Specific Objectives

- To increase the use of clean technologies in day production.
- To provide access to financial services to make the transition to low-carbon, climate-resilient, and sustainable energy production and use.

## Theory of Change, Assumptions and Risks

The theory of change is based on the need to strengthen systems and institutional capacities that allow for increased efficiency and reduced emissions. The programme will achieve its objectives through:

- Increased access to clean technologies.
- Increased access to financial services.
- Increased capacity to adopt and implement GASI emission reduction measures.

### Assumptions

The programme assumes that GASI components and activities, supplemented by targeted interventions for women and youth to increase the specific barriers identified. This approach will enable programme participants to achieve their objectives through increased efficiency and reduced emissions while also addressing the long-term goals of barriers very likely to include:

- The GASI theory of change is based on the need to strengthen systems and institutional capacities that allow for increased efficiency and reduced emissions.
- The programme assumes that GASI components and activities, supplemented by targeted interventions for women and youth to increase the specific barriers identified. This approach will enable programme participants to achieve their objectives through increased efficiency and reduced emissions while also addressing the long-term goals of barriers very likely to include:

## SUB-ACTIVITY 1.1.3.1 SUPPORT THE REVIEW AND PREPARATION OF NATIONAL DAIRY POLICIES, STRATEGIES, AND REGULATIONS TO ENSURE CHANGE ADAPTATION

### Activity 1.1.3.1.1 Review and facilitate dairy better policies, strategies, and regulations to date change adaptation in the dairy sector

This sub-activity will review and facilitate existing national policies, strategies, and regulations that have been implemented in response to changes in the dairy sector. The activity will also review and facilitate the development of new policies, strategies, and regulations that are needed to support the dairy sector's ability to adapt to change. This includes reviewing and facilitating the development of new policies, strategies, and regulations that are needed to support the dairy sector's ability to adapt to change.

### Sub-activity 1.1.3.1 Support the review and preparation of national dairy policies, strategies, and regulations to ensure change adaptation

This sub-activity will support the review and preparation of national dairy policies, strategies, and regulations to ensure change adaptation in the dairy sector. # Nutrition of Older Adults Contingency and Contingency Plans

Utilizing the South Florida Area Development Strategy and Community Action Plan, the Department of Elder Affairs (DOEA) is committed to enhancing the quality of life for older adults in Florida through a variety of programs and services. These include:

- **Nutrition Services:** Providing meals, nutrition education, and support for community-based organizations that address food insecurity.
- **Healthy Aging:** Promoting healthy aging initiatives, including falls prevention, chronic disease self-management, and health promotion.
- **Caregiver Support:** Offering respite care, support groups, and educational resources to caregivers of older adults.

## Sub-activity 1.1.3

Support the regional policy dialogue to mainstream climate change adaptation and mitigation efforts into nutrition services for older adults.

### Objectives
- Develop a regional FAO assessment and propose further regional alignment on disaster risk reduction (DRR) and climate change adaptation (CCA).
- Integrate DRR and CCA into nutrition service delivery, including emergency food assistance.
- Enhance the capacity of local governments to address climate-related risks in nutrition services.

## Sub-activity 1.1.3 National Disaster Plan Preparation and Response

The aim of this activity is to enhance the ability of DOEA to prepare for and respond to the impacts of climate change on older adults. This includes:

- Developing a national disaster plan that addresses the unique needs of older adults.
- Establishing partnerships with local governments, community-based organizations, and other stakeholders to ensure effective response and recovery efforts.

## Sub-activity 1.1.4 Disaster/Risk/Reduced/Food Safety

Launch awareness campaigns on nutritional benefits of old and food safety aspects. This will be a long-term report on risk communication and nutrition messages, accompanied by activities education and social media campaigns. The aim is to improve the nutritional status of older adults in the school of government programs. The aim is to improve nutritional vulnerability groups, consumption of risk reduction and health promotion. The aim is to improve the nutritional status of older adults in the school of government programs. The aim is to improve nutritional vulnerability groups, consumption of risk reduction and health promotion.

### Objectives
- Develop and implement nutrition education campaigns targeting older adults.
- Promote healthy eating habits and food safety practices among older adults.
- Collaborate with local governments and community-based organizations to enhance nutrition services for older adults.

# Sub-activity 1.1.2.1 Health awareness on the consequences of milk in nutrition and health

The focus will be on increasing awareness among women on the importance of milk in their diet and that of their children. This will include activities such as:

- Conducting awareness campaigns.
- Distributing leaflets and posters.
- Organizing group discussions.

# Sub-activity 1.1.2.2 Bulk capacity of dairy ingestion to ensure milk quality and safety and reduce waste

The focus will be on increasing the bulk capacity of dairy ingestion among women, children, and other vulnerable groups. This will include activities such as:

- Conducting awareness campaigns.
- Distributing leaflets and posters.
- Organizing group discussions.

# Activity 1.1.3 Integrated leisure-time activity for sustainable dairy farming

The programme will aim to address a lack of knowledge among women on the importance of integrated leisure-time activities in sustainable dairy farming. The focus will be on:

- Conducting awareness campaigns.
- Distributing leaflets and posters.
- Organizing group discussions.

# Sub-activity 1.1.3.1 Mapping households and groups for planning

The focus will be on mapping households and groups to plan for the programme to target women, children, and other vulnerable groups. This will include activities such as:

- Conducting household surveys.
- Mapping households and groups.
- Organizing group discussions.

# Sub-activity 1.1.3.2 Developing capacity in sustainable dairy farming

The focus will be on capacity building of local authorities and farming associations to support the programme. This will include activities such as:

- Conducting training sessions.
- Providing technical assistance.
- Facilitating access to resources for women, youth, and other vulnerable groups.

# Sub-activity 1.1.3.3 Participatory planning of leisure-time activity

The focus will be on participatory planning of leisure-time activities with communities and at the local level. This will include activities such as:

- Conducting community meetings.
- Facilitating group discussions.
- Developing a plan for the programme.

# Objectives

## Objective 1: Improve vaccine efficacy to the dairy sector

Activity 1.3.1: Minimize emergency services for dairy cattle health, higher calf survival and reduced disease. A low level of infectious diseases is essential for healthy and productive animals. Climate change exacerbates the emergence of new emerging diseases and infections (such as Clostridium perfringens, Salmonella spp., Mycoplasma bovis, etc.). The objective is to improve the management of dairy cattle health by reducing the impact of infectious diseases on productivity. The right measures provide a basis for the development of preventive strategies that can be implemented in the field among the women and youth in the households. This requires the research and capacity to veterinary services, the development of new vaccines and the improvement of existing ones.

## Objective 2: Support the establishment of local private and public veterinarians and veterinary paraprofessionals

Activity 1.3.2: Support the establishment of local private and public veterinarians and veterinary paraprofessionals. The long-term objective is to improve the management of dairy cattle health and disease control, using the “one health” approach. This approach will have three main components: (i) support private veterinarians and para-veterinarians; (ii) support the development of a network of local private and public veterinary paraprofessionals; and (iii) provide adequate training to local farmers, youth and women in animal husbandry practices. The objective is to improve the management of dairy cattle health and disease control by providing adequate training to local farmers, youth and women in animal husbandry practices.

# Sub-activity 1.2.3.1 Preparation of testing modules for the extended climate ignition and extinction measures

The preparation of testing modules that are part of the proposed activities under sub-activity 1.2.6 and related CC will be carried out in close collaboration with the relevant laboratories (e.g., FZK, DLR, and others). The development of these modules will include the following: ignition and extinction conditions, including ignition and extinction limits; ignition and extinction times; ignition and extinction temperatures; and other parameters that are relevant to the assessment of the extended climate ignition and extinction measures. These modules will be prepared in accordance with the requirements of the Programme and will support through, for example, FZK, the development of the extended climate ignition and extinction measures.

# Sub-activity 1.2.3.2 Preparation of testing modules for (RMI) and facilities of extended systems to climate change related impacts

The objective is to support the strengthening of a research (FZK) as the establishment of a +15% (paling at least) increase in the number of research staff and post-doctoral researchers. The preparation of testing modules will include the following: ignition and extinction conditions, including ignition and extinction limits; ignition and extinction times; ignition and extinction temperatures; and other parameters that are relevant to the assessment of the extended climate ignition and extinction measures.

# Sub-activity 1.3.3: Collect activity data on farm to calculate the daily emissions under various head, animal and feed categories.

This sub-activity will collect data from a sample of farms with respect to emission sources, including enteric fermentation and manure management, using light footing, direct farm-specific data sets. The methods data on emissions will be collected through questionnaires and interviews. This sub-activity will be performed in close collaboration with the national research institutes and universities. The data will be used to calculate the daily emissions under various head, animal and feed categories. This activity will also allow the Programme's staff, other staff and students to understand and develop their skills.

This sub-activity will include climate change impact assessments, including impacts on the dairy sector. The Programme will also collect data on the carbon footprint of the farms in the programme area. This activity will be performed in close collaboration with the national research institutes and universities.

# Sub-activity 1.A.3.1: Support to regional centers of excellence in technology validation and dissemination

The sub-activity will support the development of regional centers of excellence in technology validation and dissemination. These centers will provide a platform for the exchange of knowledge, best practices, and lessons learned among researchers, innovators, and practitioners. The centers will also facilitate the adoption of technologies by local communities, including youth and women, and help improve their livelihoods through innovation and commercialization of new products, services, and processes. This will contribute to the achievement of national development goals.

# Sub-activity 1.A.3.2: Developing and maintaining regional knowledge platforms on agriculture, GIS, extension, and related technologies

This sub-activity will support the development, maintenance, and promotion of regional knowledge platforms on agriculture, GIS, extension, and related technologies. These platforms will provide a repository for best practices, lessons learned, and success stories from various countries in the region. The platforms will also facilitate the exchange of information among stakeholders, including farmers, researchers, and policymakers.

# Activity 1.A.3: Establish regional cooperation patterns and participation in global networks for technology validation and dissemination

The activity will establish regional cooperation patterns and participation in global networks for technology validation and dissemination. This will involve various partners and private sector actors, including the EAC, AU, SMED, SADC, LAS, the East African Community (EAC), and other relevant organizations. The activity will also support the development of regional knowledge platforms and networks, such as the One Health Research and Education Consortium (OHREC) and the Global Forum on Agricultural Research (GFAR).

# Sub-activity 1.A.3.3: Strengthening capacity in technology validation and dissemination

This sub-activity will support the development of capacity in technology validation and dissemination. This will involve training, mentoring, and coaching for researchers, innovators, and practitioners in the region. The activity will also support the establishment of regional networks and partnerships to facilitate the exchange of knowledge and best practices.

# Sub-activity 1.A.3.4: Developing a regional plan of action

This sub-activity will develop a regional plan of action for technology validation and dissemination. This plan will be based on a comprehensive assessment of the needs and priorities of the region, including the identification of key stakeholders, technologies, and challenges. The plan will also include strategies for the promotion and adoption of new technologies.

# Sub-activity 1.A.3.5: Facilitating access to global knowledge platforms

This sub-activity will facilitate access to global knowledge platforms on agriculture, GIS, extension, and related technologies. This will involve the development of regional networks and partnerships with international organizations, such as the FAO, IFAD, and the World Bank. The activity will also support the participation of regional stakeholders in global events and conferences.

# Sub-activity 1.A.3.6: Promoting regional cooperation

This sub-activity will promote regional cooperation in technology validation and dissemination. This will involve the development of regional networks and partnerships with international organizations, such as the FAO, IFAD, and the World Bank. The activity will also support the participation of regional stakeholders in global events and conferences.

# Sub-activity 2.3.1 Assess the need and utility of renewable energy and waste management solutions

The aim will be to assess the clean energy needs of dairy operations, and propose RE solutions (photovoltaics, PC, biogas) that are economically viable and technically feasible. The assessment will include a cost-benefit analysis for each solution.

# Sub-activity 2.3.1.1 Support the adoption of renewable energy and waste management solutions

This would include preparation or supporting of dairy operations with technical RE, improved technologies and new concepts to reduce their carbon footprint. This could be achieved through training, better financial and other support. Solar energy will continue the existing activities from national and regional projects. The aim is to increase the use of renewable energy and bio-waste management for use in on-farm and dairy value chain activities.

# Sub-activity 2.3.1.2 Optimize energy loading in energy efficiency and waste management

This training will focus on the management of RE solutions in dairy operations, MML and MECL. This includes training for reuse of waste products from dairy operations to produce renewable energy and bio-waste management for use in on-farm and dairy value chain activities.

# Sub-activity 2.3.1.3 Promote the adoption of climate-smart dairy models adapted to local change, changing and growing environments

This will help in improving productivity and water utilization of dairy operations. The adoption of climate-smart practices will promote resilience of dairy operations to climate change. This includes adaptation to such changes as increased temperatures, and capacity to clean change.

# Sub-activity 2.3.1.4 Assess the need for and feasibility of climate-smart dairy models adapted to local change, changing and growing environments

This will help in improving productivity and water utilization of dairy operations. The adoption of climate-smart practices will promote resilience of dairy operations to climate change. This includes adaptation to such changes as increased temperatures, and capacity to clean change.

# Sub-activity 2.1.3 Maintain good quality semen for chicken meat breeders, adapt to a semen dose

Sub-activity 2.1.3.1 Maintain good quality semen for chicken meat breeders, adapt to a semen dose. The main objective is to maintain the genetic potential of small-scale poultry farmers by providing them with high-quality semen. This will be achieved through the following steps: (i) identification and selection of breeder birds; (ii) collection and preservation of semen; (iii) training of farmers on artificial insemination techniques; and (iv) provision of extension services to support the adoption of these technologies. The activity will target small-scale poultry farmers, particularly those in rural areas.

Sub-activity 2.1.3.2 Invest in breeding and AI centres. To achieve betterments in AI and embryo transfer, the Programme will invest in the establishment of breeding and AI centres. These centres will be equipped with the latest technology to ensure the availability of good-quality semen, storage, and quality control equipment. The centres will also provide training for farmers on the use of these technologies. The centres will be located in areas where there is a high demand for poultry products.

Sub-activity 2.1.3.3 Make the implementation of every activity to make the breed more productive and disease resistant. This sub-activity will focus on the following: (i) selection of breeding stock; (ii) improvement of management practices; (iii) provision of extension services; and (iv) promotion of the use of modern technologies.

Sub-activity 2.1.3.4 Digitate awareness campaigns and support breeder organizations. This activity will facilitate the dissemination of information on the benefits of using high-quality semen and the importance of maintaining good hygiene practices in poultry production.

# Sub-activity 2.2.2 Provide support for better animal care, health management and disease prevention

East Africa, including diseases and parasites that affect primary sources of animal products and products used in human nutrition, is a major area of concern. The region has a high prevalence of zoonotic diseases such as brucellosis, tuberculosis, anthrax, and trypanosomiasis. These diseases affect people's livelihoods and the economy by reducing productivity and causing illness and death. The region also faces challenges in providing support for animal welfare, which is increasingly recognized as an important component of sustainable development. Although they may be new to the area, these groups will continue to play a major role in this sub-activity.

# Sub-activity 2.3.1 Maintain existing forage and related pastures to promote and sustainably multiply forages

Sub-activity 2.3.1.1 Support multiplication of forage crops and pasture species. This sub-activity will support the multiplication of forage crops and pasture species for sustainable use, enhancing biodiversity and soil fertility. It will also support the conservation of forage and related pastures.

Sub-activity 2.3.1.2 Support multiplication of elite molecular seeds for fodder crops and pastures. This sub-activity will support the multiplication of elite molecular seeds for fodder crops and pasture species. It will enhance biodiversity, sustainable approaches to multiple seeds and make them available to 15%, 40% farmers and other groups.

Sub-activity 2.3.1.3 Build capacity of dairy farmers on forage production and conservation on farmers' fields and semi-intensive systems. This sub-activity will support the capacity building of dairy farmers on forage production and conservation. The IFFT will focus on (i) training on good forage management practices, i.e., grass and legume species selection, seed treatment, sowing methods, crop establishment, maintenance, harvesting, and utilization; (ii) demonstration of mechanisms of forage production with fodder chopper/cutter, haymaking, silage making, etc.; and (iii) capacity building on the use of improved forages.

Sub-activity 2.3.1.4 Access to water for animal and forage production in drought-prone intense dairy systems. The Programme will provide micro-irrigation equipment for sustainable harvesting and saving of individual farm waters, including rainwater harvesting, and water conservation techniques. It will also support the establishment of small reservoirs and water storage facilities.

Activity 2.3.1 Promote improved natural feeding practices to reduce ethnic methods emission. Appropriate dry fodder formulation is key to improve animal welfare, while reducing methane (CH4) emissions from enteric fermentation. The Programme will promote improved natural feeding practices, including better nutrition for animals and manure use in crop production systems.

Sub-activity 2.3.1.5 Build capacity on appropriate feed formulation. In each county, the Programme will support the establishment of demonstration farms to train farmers on sustainable natural feed formulations with a total area of 40 hectares (HA) to enhance productivity efficiency.

Sub-activity 2.3.1.6 Promote appropriate feed formulation that reduces methane emission. In four counties, the IFFT and the dairy cooperatives will be the vehicle to reach out to dairy farmers and promote improved natural feeding practices.

# Reproductive Beeding of Dairy Cattle

The Programme will be 10 months long and is open to all women in LIPAs who are interested in learning about reproductive management of dairy cattle. The programme will include an introduction to the use of artificial insemination (AI) techniques with emphasis on the use of sexed-sorted semen, heat detection, oestrus synchronization, and embryo transfer. The programme will also cover the basics of herd health management, including vaccination protocols, parasite control, and general animal husbandry practices.

## Sub-activity 1.3.1 Household sustainable nutrient recycling of manure and daily waste to reduce environmental pollution

Women in LIPAs will be trained on how to manage their household waste and manure to reduce environmental pollution. The training will include the use of composting techniques, biogas production, and other methods for reducing waste. The programme will also provide information on how to properly dispose of hazardous materials and how to recycle waste products.

## Sub-activity 1.3.2 Household sustainable nutrient recycling of manure and daily waste to reduce environmental pollution

Women in LIPAs will be trained on how to manage their household waste and manure to reduce environmental pollution. The training will include the use of composting techniques, biogas production, and other methods for reducing waste. The programme will also provide information on how to properly dispose of hazardous materials and how to recycle waste products.

## Sub-activity 1.3.3 More women involved in livestock farming

The Programme will create awareness and build capacity to increase the number of women involved in livestock farming. The programme will include training on animal husbandry, breeding techniques, and marketing strategies for women farmers. The programme will also provide information on how to access credit and other financial services to support their businesses.

## Sub-activity 1.3.4 Promote adapted animal housing

The Programme will promote adapted animal housing with the aim of improving the health and productivity of livestock. The programme will include training on the design and construction of appropriate housing for different types of animals.

## Sub-activity 1.3.5 More women engaged in extended systems production and applied during grazing season

The Programme will train LIPAs on the EFRUPs for promoting more effective farm community-based for use, feed, and income generation. The programme will also provide training on the management of extended systems production and applied during grazing season for better animal management and resource conservation. The activity will target to support women and poor landowners.

## Sub-activity 1.3.6 Build capacity for on-farm use of biofuels and biogas

The Programme will build capacity for on-farm use of biofuels and biogas. The programme will include training on the production of biofuels and biogas from agricultural waste, as well as the use of these fuels in rural communities. The programme will also provide information on how to access financing for the construction of biofuel and biogas facilities.

It will also support the establishment of environmental committees, water committees, fire committees, local government, district and national levels to ensure effective implementation of the programme. This will include training in participatory approaches, including gender mainstreaming, conflict resolution, and resource management.

## Sub-activity 3.2.1: Water availability

Dependent on the country, is enhanced access to safe for daily usage a minimum of 50 liters per person per day. The programme will invest in water harvesting systems such as boreholes, small-scale dykes, and rainwater harvesting tanks. It will also support the rehabilitation of existing water points and the construction of new ones. The programme will also enhance the capacity of communities to manage water resources sustainably. This includes promoting integrated water resource management (IWRM) and supporting community-based natural resource management (CBNRM). The programme will also promote gender equality in water management, including ensuring equal access to water for women and men.

The programme will invest in water harvesting systems such as boreholes, small-scale dykes, and rainwater harvesting tanks. It will also support the rehabilitation of existing water points and the construction of new ones. The programme will also enhance the capacity of communities to manage water resources sustainably. This includes promoting integrated water resource management (IWRM) and supporting community-based natural resource management (CBNRM). The programme will also promote gender equality in water management, including ensuring equal access to water for women and men.

### Activity 3.1.1 Identify eligible businesses for investment

While the roll-out of developing a clearinghouse will focus on small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs), it is important to note that the Green Dayy Financing Facility (GDF) will also consider larger businesses by making general and sub-sectoral provisions for the clearinghouse. The GDF will identify eligible businesses through a combination of direct outreach, screening of investment proposals, and monitoring of investment outcomes. This screening framework will be underpinned by a scoring system that evaluates the eligibility of investment proposals based on their alignment with the GDF’s objectives and environmental and social safeguards.

### Sub-Activity 3.1.2.1 Prepare documentation and convene

The objective for the GDF is to conduct thorough due diligence on potential investees, which will include a comprehensive review of business plans, financial statements, and other relevant documents. The GDF will also review prospective investee projects against the defined screening criteria, which will ultimately ensure and support eligible businesses.

### Activity 3.1.3 Close smart financing products as soon as possible

The GDF will provide financing to small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) and renewable energy projects through various financial instruments. The GDF will focus on providing financing to SMEs that are involved in the development of clean technologies, such as solar power, wind power, and other renewable energy sources. The GDF will also work with local financial institutions to provide financing to small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) that are involved in the development of clean technologies.

## Gender equality and social inclusion

### Component 1: Programme management

Aimed to strengthen the GESI dimension of programme management.

- Support for GESI Action Plan implementation will be provided from the Technical Assistance, which includes capacity building at different levels including for implementing partners and end beneficiaries.
- A GESI focal point for each cluster will be nominated based on the roll out of the GESI action plan for each country. This person will be responsible to ensure that all activities are gender sensitive and inclusive and will work closely with the Monitoring and Evaluation team to ensure that GESI indicators are captured in the community level data.
- Include a GESI component in the annual work plans and budgets aligning the terms of reference of the implementation teams from partner organizations. This will be backed by capacity strengthening on gender mainstreaming and addressing social inclusion issues at different levels including through UBBF and GESI goals.
- Select implementing partners (government and non-government) with a proven commitment to community engagement, gender equality, and social inclusion. Ensure that these partners are not only committed but also have the capacity to deliver the expected results and outputs. The implementing agencies should be held accountable for delivering on their targets.
- Ensure that annual work plans and budgets are prepared aligned with a gender perspective, and include the necessary resources to ensure effective implementation of GESI activities. This includes ensuring that GESI goals are properly resourced, monitored, and evaluated (including indicators) and that staff and pay between genders is equitable.
- Ensure that senior management of GESI considerations, including attention to opportunities and expertise in this programming area, is reflected in the programme design and implementation process.

### Monitoring, evaluation and knowledge management unit

- Support for planned and additional gender and social norms assessments for the daily interactions where the project is implemented. This will be aligned with the alignment of the overall GESI strategy and IRM implementation progress. Strategies to increase the inclusion of women, youth, and people with disabilities are highlighted as a priority. The programme will also include a focus on ensuring that gender equality and social inclusion monitoring parameters are developed in line with the objectives of the project.

# GENDER AND SOCIAL INCLUSION ACTION PLAN MATRIX

## Impact Statement

If technological solutions for reducing methane and other greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions and increasing climate resilience are achieved by days farmers and herders, women and youth, and indigenous peoples, it will contribute to the achievement of the Paris Agreement targets. The protection of these groups is critical as they are often the most vulnerable to climate change impacts. Gender-responsive solutions will ensure that women and girls have equal access to resources and opportunities.

## Outcome Statement

DAMA will ensure climate change related data enterprise Aspen Networks technologies with the benefits of land and human security for women, youth, and indigenous peoples. DAMA will also promote gender equality in the workplace by ensuring that women are represented at all levels of the organization. DAMA will achieve this through the implementation of a comprehensive gender equality policy, including training and awareness programs to address unconscious bias and gender stereotypes. DAMA will also work with market households and host for underdeveloped day programs and policies through their own efforts for a new environment, climate change, and gender equality. DAMA will also promote gender equality in the workplace by ensuring that women are represented at all levels of the organization.

The overall budget required to implement the GEI initiative DAMA agenda is estimated to be USD 2 million.

# Activity 1.2 Launch awareness campaigns on nutritional benefits of milk and dairy products

Launch awareness campaigns on nutritional benefits of milk and dairy products, accompanied by nutrition education on safe handling, storage and consumption of milk and dairy products. The campaign will be implemented in collaboration with the Ministry of Health and Child Care (MoHCC) and other stakeholders such as schools, churches and milk vendors in the whole milk program. The aim is to enhance knowledge and understanding of the nutritional benefits of milk and dairy products among school children and milk handlers in the whole milk program. The campaign will be implemented through various media platforms including radio, television, print media, social media, community meetings, and consumption of high-quality and safe dairy products. The campaign will also include a nutrition education component (NEC) that has been rolled out to inform key stakeholders on milk safety, risk management and food safety.

## Sub-activity 1.2.1 Public awareness on the contribution of milk and dairy products

Public awareness on the contribution of milk and dairy products to health and well-being will be carried out through various community platforms such as schools, LPLP, churches, and other community gatherings. The campaign will include improved milk treatment, food safety and nutrition messages of dairy in households. It will include women, youth participation in dairy farming under the whole milk program. The campaign will also promote and disseminate dairy products. Tanzania, Uganda and Kenya will be the main target countries for this sub-activity.

## Sub-activity 1.2.2 Build capacity of dairy inspectors to ensure the quality of milk and dairy products

Build capacity of dairy inspectors to ensure the quality of milk and dairy products through training on food safety standards, quality control and risk management. The program will include technical training and equipment for women and men in charge of milk handling and processing. The campaign will enhance knowledge and understanding of milk safety and quality standards. Gender mainstreaming will be emphasized throughout the campaign to ensure equal participation and retention of inspectors. Women and youth will be supported to attend, travel to and from training.

# Activity 1.3.3

Improves and ensures security for sustainable air traffic management. The activity will support the development of a comprehensive safety management system (SMS) that includes risk assessment, mitigation, and accident/incident reporting. This activity will also support the development of a culture of safety and the implementation of safety promotion planning and management. In addition, it will support the development of a safety management system for air traffic controllers and associated personnel, the focus will be on the identification of safety risks and the development of mitigation measures to address these risks. The project will include capacity building and training activities in the field of safety management and accident/incident investigation and will use local level capacities.

## Objective 1.3 Improved service delivery to the dairy sector

### Sub-objective 1.3.1 Support of improved animal health, higher milk yields and reduced losses.

- Increased access to quality feed and veterinary services.
- Disease control through vaccination.

- It will enhance the capacity of the dairy sector and livestock value chains.

# Sub-activity 1.2.1 Preparation of training modules

The training modules will be developed through a consultative process involving relevant stakeholders and experts in the field of maternal, newborn and child health (MNCH). The modules will cover key topics such as antenatal care, safe delivery, postnatal care, and newborn care. They will also include case studies and role-plays to enhance the learning experience.

## Output 1.2.1

Preparation of training modules for MNCH (including LFP and FFS) (100%)

# Sub-activity 1.2.2 Training of master trainers

Master trainers will be identified from among existing health workers with relevant experience in maternal, newborn and child health services. They will undergo a comprehensive training program that covers all aspects of the LFP and FFS modules. The training will include both theoretical and practical components to ensure that the master trainers are well-equipped to train other health workers.

## Output 1.2.2

Training of master trainers for MNCH (including LFP and FFS) (100%)

# Sub-activity 1.3.1 Capacity assessment and development of training materials

A comprehensive capacity assessment will be conducted to identify the existing skills and knowledge gaps among health workers in maternal, newborn and child health services. Based on this assessment, training materials will be developed to address these gaps.

## Output 1.3.1

Capacity assessment and development of training materials for MNCH (including LFP and FFS) (100%)

# Sub-activity 1.2.3 Training of frontline health workers through LFP and FFS

Frontline health workers will be trained using the developed modules by the master trainers. The training will be conducted in batches to ensure that all health workers receive adequate training. The training will include both theoretical and practical components.

## Output 1.2.3

Training of frontline health workers through LFP and FFS (including LFP and FFS) (100%)

# Sub-activity 1.3.2 Capacity development and implementation of guidelines

Based on the capacity assessment, a comprehensive plan will be developed to address any identified gaps in knowledge and skills among health workers. This plan will include the development and implementation of guidelines for maternal, newborn and child health services.

## Output 1.3.2

Capacity development and implementation of guidelines for MNCH (including LFP and FFS) (100%)

# Sub-activity 2.2.3

Sub-activity 2.2.3 aims to use the combination of day care facilities to make the best use of women's productive and reproductive time. The Programme will train all technicians, supervisors and field workers in the use of day care centres. District barrack officers (at least 80 percent men and 40 percent women), district health officers and other relevant personnel will be trained in the aspects of the organisation between day care facilities and existing maternal and child health services, including breastfeeding, hygiene and nutrition. In collaboration with the Women's League, local leaders will be trained to organise and supervise day care centres. The Programme will also provide all technicians, supervisors and field workers with training in the use of day care facilities. In addition, the Programme will train women who are not available for employment in the existing health services to work as day care assistants. In Uganda, most of the technicians will be trained by the Ministry of Health. The Programme will also provide training for local leaders and community members who are not available.

## Campaigns and support "breeder" organisations

The campaigns will target men, women and youth in order to change attitudes towards the use of family planning methods. This campaign will include awareness-raising on the use of family planning methods, the importance of the husband's role, and the need for both partners to be involved in decision-making about family planning. The campaign will also provide training for local leaders with a view to changing community attitudes towards family planning. Campaigns will also give guidance to "breeder" organisations and support for their activities.

## Action 2.2.3

### Prepare for future demand

This action aims to prepare for future demand and make the best use of existing facilities. The Programme will train all technicians, supervisors and field workers in the use of day care centres. District barrack officers (at least 80 percent men and 40 percent women), district health officers and other relevant personnel will be trained in the aspects of the organisation between day care facilities and existing maternal and child health services, including breastfeeding, hygiene and nutrition. In collaboration with the Women's League, local leaders will be trained to organise and supervise day care centres. The Programme will also provide all technicians, supervisors and field workers with training in the use of day care facilities. In addition, the Programme will train women who are not available for employment in the existing health services to work as day care assistants. In Uganda, most of the technicians will be trained by the Ministry of Health. The Programme will also provide training for local leaders and community members who are not available.

### Campaigns and support "breeder" organisations

The campaigns will target men, women and youth in order to change attitudes towards the use of family planning methods. This campaign will include awareness-raising on the use of family planning methods, the importance of the husband's role, and the need for both partners to be involved in decision-making about family planning. The campaign will also provide training for local leaders with a view to changing community attitudes towards family planning. Campaigns will also give guidance to "breeder" organisations and support for their activities.

# Sub-activity 2.2.1

Sub-activity 2.2.1 seeks to reduce the use of diesel gensets in monitor rural health and community centers by promoting the use of solar PV systems. This will be achieved through:

- Provision of solar PV systems.
- Training on proper maintenance, repair, and management.
- Use of the grid as a backup power source.

This sub-activity will also be linked to the activities under the energy component of the project. It will augment the use of diesel gensets in the rural health units. To implement this activity, the following strategies are proposed:

- Promotion of solar PV systems for rural health centers.
- Training on proper maintenance and repair of solar PV systems.
- Advocacy on the benefits of using renewable energy sources.

## Activity 2.3.1

Activity 2.3.1 promotes clean fuel production and consumption. The main objective is to reduce emissions from traditional biomass use in cooking, heating, and lighting. This will be achieved through:

- Introduction of improved cookstoves.
- Promotion of cleaner fuels such as LPG (Liquefied Petroleum Gas).
- Public awareness campaigns on the benefits of using clean fuel.

### Sub-activity 2.3.1.A

Sub-activity 2.3.1.A focuses on promoting the use of LPG for cooking and heating in rural areas. This will be achieved through:

- Provision of LPG stoves.
- Training on proper usage and maintenance of LPG stoves.
- Promotion campaigns to encourage the shift from traditional biomass to LPG.

### Sub-activity 2.3.1.B

Sub-activity 2.3.1.B focuses on promoting the use of improved cookstoves for cooking in rural areas. This will be achieved through:

- Provision of improved cookstoves.
- Training on proper usage and maintenance of improved cookstoves.
- Promotion campaigns to encourage the shift from traditional biomass to improved cookstoves.

## Activity 2.3.2

Activity 2.3.2 promotes energy efficiency in rural health centers. The main objective is to reduce energy consumption through simple and cost-effective measures. This will be achieved through:

- Installation of energy-efficient lighting.
- Use of solar-powered water heaters.
- Promotion of energy-saving practices.

### Sub-activity 2.3.2.A

Sub-activity 2.3.2.A focuses on promoting the use of energy-efficient lighting in rural health centers. This will be achieved through:

- Provision of energy-efficient light bulbs.
- Training on proper usage and maintenance of energy-efficient light bulbs.
- Promotion campaigns to encourage the shift from traditional lighting to energy-efficient lighting.

### Sub-activity 2.3.2.B

Sub-activity 2.3.2.B focuses on promoting the use of solar-powered water heaters in rural health centers. This will be achieved through:

- Provision of solar-powered water heaters.
- Training on proper usage and maintenance of solar-powered water heaters.
- Promotion campaigns to encourage the shift from traditional water heating methods to solar-powered water heaters.

# Activity 3.1.2 Identify eligible businesses for investment

While this will focus on women-owned businesses, it is important to note that the activities and benefits described here apply equally to men and female owners. This activity will ensure sustainable outcomes and long-term success by providing training and support to women entrepreneurs.

## Activity 3.1.2.1 Business Management Training

Establish a business management training framework to support connections in investment readiness. This includes:

- Providing training on business management.
- Establishing linkages with potential investors.
- Providing mentorship and coaching for women-owned businesses.
- Ensuring that the training is tailored to the needs of women entrepreneurs.
